# Documentation update log

## 2026-09-22

- **Update** [Exit codes of a headless run](cli/run-exit-codes.md) and [`namzu run-stream`](cli/run-stream.md): a signal that finds the turn already settled, while the session is still closing, no longer reports it as left interrupted, and `run-stream` writes one `done`, the turn's own, after its `terminated` error; a signal while `run --wait-for-provider` waits says the turn is paused at its checkpoint and does not resume it (`packages/cli/src/commands/run.ts`, `packages/cli/src/commands/run-stream.ts`).
- **Update** [Session log](sdk/session-log.md) gains "A writer that stops": `releaseHeldSessionLeases` and `SessionLeasesReleasedError` (`packages/sdk/src/store/session-log/held-leases.ts`), why a turn stopped that way is left `interrupted`, and why an expired lease rather than a check that the holder's pid is gone frees one left by SIGKILL. [Exit codes of a headless run](cli/run-exit-codes.md), [`namzu run-stream`](cli/run-stream.md) and [Session storage](cli/session-storage.md): the TUI, `run` and `run-stream` give the conversation's lease back on SIGTERM, SIGHUP and SIGINT and die of the signal (`packages/cli/src/termination.ts`), so `/abandon`, `/resume` and the next prompt no longer wait up to five minutes; `run-stream` ends with an `error` event of `code: "terminated"` and a `done`.
- **Update** [Session storage](cli/session-storage.md) and [Session log](sdk/session-log.md) list the `lease.<fence>.json` files that sit beside `lease.json` in every session directory, child sessions' included (`packages/sdk/src/store/session-log/lease.ts`); the pages had shown `lease.json` alone.
- **Update** [File checkpoints](cli/file-checkpoints.md): a turn's `/restore` records go to the `file-history/` of the conversation the turn belongs to; after `/resume` they had gone to the conversation the process started in, leaving an empty `<session-id>/` with no log under `projects/<slug>/`.
- **Update** [Conversation evidence search](cli/conversation-evidence.md), [Retained tool evidence](sdk/retained-tool-evidence.md), [Automatic conversation evidence recall](sdk/evidence-recall.md) and [Request-only step context](sdk/step-context.md): a running turn's `captureSessionEvidence` covers the session log up to the turn's latest record, earlier turns included, so `search_conversation`, `read_conversation` and automatic recall inside a turn reach what earlier turns recorded; a search narrowed by `turnId` reads the log as a snapshot. The search page now describes the CLI's one reader, its paging bounds and its result fields (`unavailable`, not `scannedTurns`/`unavailableTurns`) as `packages/cli/src/integrations/sessions/conversation-search.ts` implements them.
- **Update** [Where tools run](cli/tool-boundary.md), [Adding a directory](cli/add-dir.md), [Crossing the tool boundary](sdk/escalations.md) and [The review policy](sdk/review-policy.md): `plan` mode asks about a batch of reads that reaches outside the roots instead of refusing it; a `deny` rule that refuses a whole batch still records each crossing as refused; `/add-dir` stores the canonical path it asked about and no longer adds a directory inside the working directory.
- **Update** [Where tools run](cli/tool-boundary.md), [Crossing the tool boundary](sdk/escalations.md), [The review policy](sdk/review-policy.md) and [Adding a directory](cli/add-dir.md): a path outside the roots is asked about in every mode, `auto` and an earlier "allow all" included, and refused with nobody to ask (`OUTSIDE_ROOTS_UNATTENDED_REFUSAL`); an approved `write` of a new file outside the roots succeeds; a refused crossing is an audit record with outcome `refused`; `/add-dir` decides inside or outside after following links; the sandbox's description says what `bwrap` still mounts.
- **Creation** [Where tools run](cli/tool-boundary.md): the CLI runs tools on the host under the permission system by default; a file tool's path outside the working directory is an approval request; the OS sandbox is opt-in (`sandbox.enabled: true`) and a sandboxed `bash` call may leave it for one command only with an approval asked every time (`sandbox.allowEscape`, `sandbox.allowUnattendedEscape`); what the model is told, including under WSL.
- **Creation** [Crossing the tool boundary](sdk/escalations.md): `QueryParams.outsideRootAccess` and `sandboxEscape`, `ToolCallSummary.escalation`, `confirmedEscalations`, `ToolDefinition.pathArgument` and `sandboxEscapeArgument`, `ToolContext.approvedPaths` and `sandboxEscapeApproved`, and the `outside_root_access` and `sandbox_escape` audit records.
- **Update** [Adding a directory](cli/add-dir.md): `/add-dir` of a directory outside the working directory asks first; a path outside every root is an approval request on the host and a refusal in the sandbox.
- **Update** [The review policy](sdk/review-policy.md) says how an escalated call is reviewed; [Session log](sdk/session-log.md) lists the `approved` audit outcome; the [security report](security-report.md)'s NAMZU-SEC-007 row records the host-execution default.
- **Update** [Session log](sdk/session-log.md): `resolveSessionStorage` places a disk log by its path-derived `locator` only when that file is in the layout it was given; a `<session-id>.jsonl` kept elsewhere is placed under its `parentSessionId`, so its checkpoints no longer make a `<child-id>/` at the top of the project.
- **Update** [Session log](sdk/session-log.md): a `DiskSessionLog` built from a file path (the index's `logPath`, as the drain and `abandonTurn` open it) reads its `locator` from that path, so a child session reopened that way finds the checkpoints `DiskSessionLog.at` wrote, under its parent's `subagents/`.
- **Update** [Session log](sdk/session-log.md): a child session's checkpoints are written in its own directory under its ancestors, through `DiskSessionCheckpointStore`'s new `session` option and `DiskSessionLog.locator`, not in a stray `<child-id>/` at the top of the project.
- **Update** [Resident agents](sdk/resident-agents.md) names `receiptStatus: 'duplicate-turn'`; [Slash commands](cli/slash-commands.md) calls the `/config` section Turn limits, as the TUI does; [Resident work](cli/resident-work.md) says `start.json` links a claim to its session and turn ids; [Memory research](sdk/memory-research.md) points at `packages/sdk/src/turn/memory-recall.ts`; [the SDK index](sdk/index.md), [Tool-call budget](sdk/tool-call-budget.md), [Evidence recall](sdk/evidence-recall.md), [Framework gap audit](sdk/framework-gap-audit.md) and the Zen README say turn or child session where they still said run.
- **Update** [Delegation events](sdk/delegation-events.md): a parent on disk hands its layout to its children (`ChildSessionStorage` of kind `disk`), so child logs, meta files and `child_session_ended` land under the parent with no `paths` named anywhere.
- **Update** [`namzu drain`](cli/drain.md) is no longer a draft, and its exit codes are exact: state under `--store` that cannot be read is 1, a scope the store does not hold is 64 (`packages/cli/src/commands/drain.ts`). [`namzu run-stream`](cli/run-stream.md) is no longer a draft: the command's `sessionId`/`turnId` fields and exit 75 have landed.
- **Update** [Session task context](cli/task-context.md) and [Request-only step context](sdk/step-context.md): `PrepareStepContext.turnStartedAt` names when the turn began, and a resumed turn keeps its `turn_started` time, so tasks it closed before a pause in another process stay in "closed in this turn".
- **Update** [AG-UI clients](sdk/ag-ui.md): `AGUITurnUI` (was `AGUIRunUI`); `AGUITurnContext`, `AGUITurnOptions` and `AGUITurnUIOptions` replace the other three run-named exports.
- **Update** [The session index](sdk/sqlite-sessions.md): a host resolves a caller-side id with `resolveExternalSession`, and an A2A `contextId` with `resolveA2AContext`, both now exported from `@namzu/sdk`.

## 2026-09-21

- **Update** [Zen](sdk/zen.md) continues a conversation from `first.turn.messages`; [Run the kernel](sdk/quick-start.md)'s `paths` example is compiled by the fence gate now that `runAgent` takes `paths`.
- **Update** [Delegation events](sdk/delegation-events.md): the parent turn records `child_session_spawned` and `child_session_ended` itself, through the scheduler's optional `onChildSessionEvent`; the manager starts a child's log with `session_started.parent`.
- **Update** [Zen](sdk/zen.md): the Go catalogue carries `grok-4.7`; four ids the pages route without a models.dev entry are recorded as omissions until their limits are published.
- **Creation** [Session storage](cli/session-storage.md): every file the CLI and SDK keep under `NAMZU_HOME` — `projects/<slug>/`, one `<session-id>.jsonl` per session and what sits in `<session-id>/`, `index.sqlite`, the per-user scratch directory — and which are safe to delete. Nothing is written under the working directory.
- **Creation** [`namzu drain`](cli/drain.md), draft: one bounded pass that finds parked turns through `SessionIndex.listPendingDecisions`, claims each session's lease, continues the same turn with `resumeSession`, and keeps the 0/1/64/77 exit codes. Draft until the rebuilt command lands.
- **Creation** [`namzu run-stream`](cli/run-stream.md), draft: the NDJSON event kinds, the `sessionId` and `turnId` on `usage` and `done`, `--session` history bound as an external reference, and exit 75 with `code: "turn_in_progress"` for a session whose turn is still active. Draft until the command's new fields land.
- **Update** [Session log](sdk/session-log.md) is no longer a draft: it names the writer, reader and index, documents the token-budget document, and describes `audit` records against `types/session/audit.ts`.
- **Deprecation** [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md): the per-run layout (`runs/<id>/`, `run.json`, `messages.json`, `history/`, `report.md`, `emergency/`) no longer exists. The page now maps each old file to its place in the session layout and says what a host with an old tree does.
- **Update** [The session index](sdk/sqlite-sessions.md) replaces the `SqliteSessionStore` page: `openSessionIndex`, `SqliteSessionIndex` and `ScanSessionIndex`, what the index answers, how it detects stale logs and rebuilds, and why deleting `index.sqlite` loses nothing.
- **Update** [Token budgets](sdk/token-budgets.md): `SessionTokenBudget`, one ledger per `(rootSessionId, rootTurnId)` at `<root-session-id>/budgets/<root-turn-id>.json` (snapshot version 2); a new root turn opens its own ledger, so a changed limit is no longer a conflict.
- **Update** [Hook events](sdk/hooks.md): `turn_start`, `turn_end`, `turn_interrupt` (old names refused at load), stdin `turn_id`, `parent_session_id` and `parent_turn_id`, env `NAMZU_TURN_ID`; `session_start` and `session_end` carry no turn id.
- **Update** [Ids](sdk/ids.md): `TurnId` examples, project ids minted into `project.json` by `ensureProject`, and caller-side ids recorded as origins or external references.
- **Update** [AG-UI clients](sdk/ag-ui.md): a thread is a session and each AG-UI run a new turn; `NAMZU_TURN_ERROR`, `NAMZU_TURN_CANCELED`, `NAMZU_TURN_PAUSED`, the new `NAMZU_TURN_IN_PROGRESS`, and `namzu.turn.paused`.
- **Update** [Delegation events](sdk/delegation-events.md): a delegated agent is a child session; the parent log's `child_session_spawned` (with `batch`) and `child_session_ended`, the child's own log and meta file, and `SessionIndex.listChildren` replace the per-run `children/` directories.
- **Update** [Retained tool evidence](sdk/retained-tool-evidence.md): `createSessionEvidenceSource` binds one session log; spills live in `tool-results/`; live capture rides the log's `prev` and `prevText` links.
- **Update** [Run the kernel](sdk/quick-start.md): `runAgent` returns `turn`; the pathBuilder section becomes where the session log is recorded and how `paths` or an `InMemorySessionLog` move it.
- **Update** [Project and session state](cli/project-state.md): the project is `projects/<slug>/` with `project.json`, one active turn per session with `/resume` and `/abandon`, checkpoints without messages, and the `legacy` category of `namzu state` report version 2.
- **Update** [Exit codes of a headless run](cli/run-exit-codes.md): 75 also means the session already has an active turn.
- **Update** [Delegated work](cli/delegated-work.md): receipts and replay read child session logs, workflow and phase survive a restart as `batch`, and `/agents runs` is now `/agents batches`.
- **Update** [Conversation evidence search](cli/conversation-evidence.md), [Context and compaction](cli/context-and-compaction.md), [Memory](cli/memory.md), [Resident work](cli/resident-work.md), [Slash commands](cli/slash-commands.md), [Session task context](cli/task-context.md), [Turn limits](cli/run-limits.md) and the other CLI pages: the session log replaces run transcripts and `evidence-index/`, manual compaction is `compactSession`, memory and resident state live under `projects/<slug>/`, and `/abandon` is listed.
- **Update** Every other SDK page that said "run" for the kernel's unit of work now says turn or session, with identifiers renamed per the release notes (`captureSessionEvidence`, `SessionTextEvidenceSource`, `TurnConfig`, `SessionQuery`, `SessionEvent`); [Cancellation and timeouts](sdk/cancellation-and-timeouts.md) notes that its line citations predate the cutover.
- **Update** [Sandbox seeds](sdk/sandbox-seeds.md): a new or changed pin (from another pin or from a `ref`) must be exactly HEAD; the ancestor rule applies only to the pin the marker recorded, so a changed pin is no longer reported `present` over the old checkout.
- **Update** [Sandbox seeds](sdk/sandbox-seeds.md): a changed `ref` could still be reported `present` over the old checkout. With no record under the new ref, the check in `packages/sandbox/src/seed/index.ts` accepted the ref when its commit was an ancestor of HEAD or HEAD an ancestor of it, and the ref is often in the repository without having been checked out: a depth-2 clone of `main` carries the tag `v0` in its history, and a pinned commit's full clone carries every remote branch. Now the ref's commit must be exactly HEAD; a seed with no `ref` and no record is held to `origin/HEAD`; and the marker records a pinned repository as `:commit`, so a dropped pin no longer stands in for the default branch. Folded into `.changeset/sandbox-seeds.md`.
- **Update** [Sandbox seeds](sdk/sandbox-seeds.md), three corrections in `packages/sandbox/src/seed/index.ts`. A seed whose `ref` changed reported the old checkout `present` and rewrote the marker with the new digest over the old commit, because the recorded commit was used whatever ref recorded it; the marker now records each commit's `ref`, a record made under another ref is dropped, and the check then requires the ref in the repository and on HEAD's line of history, so a moved `ref` is drift. `defineSandboxSeed` refuses nested repository directories (`vendor` beside `vendor/lib`, which could never succeed and failed with a false "prepared by another host") and a `dir` under `.namzu/`. The marker's temporary file is named by a per-call nonce instead of the shell PID, which sandboxes sharing a disk often share across PID namespaces (7 of 400 concurrent calls failed before). Folded into `.changeset/sandbox-seeds.md`.
- **Update** [Container-tier egress](sdk/sandbox-egress.md): `egressPortsForRules`, which the `EgressProxyOptions.allowedPorts` doc names as the way to build that option from a profile's rules, was exported only from the proxy image's module and absent from `dist/index.d.ts`; it is now exported from `@namzu/sandbox`. Folded into `.changeset/sandbox-egress-proxy-ports.md`.
- **Creation** [Sandbox seeds](sdk/sandbox-seeds.md), draft, for `packages/sandbox/src/seed/index.ts`: `SandboxSeed`, `defineSandboxSeed`, `ensureSandboxSeed`, `sandboxSeedDigest` and `SandboxSeedError`. It states where `root` goes per backend, the per-call checks, why the guest-writable marker is not trusted, the URL refusals that keep credentials out of the guest, and how two hosts share one disk. [Kubernetes sandboxes](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md) points persistent workspaces at it. `src/testing/sandbox-conformance.ts` gains an opt-in `seed` case. `.changeset/sandbox-seeds.md`, **minor**.
- **Update** [Container-tier egress](sdk/sandbox-egress.md) gains a port-rules section and [Sandbox egress profiles](sdk/sandbox-egress-profiles.md) no longer refuses ports on docker and runsc. `EgressProxyOptions.allowedPorts` checks the dialled port (443 for an upgraded `http://host/` and a portless `CONNECT`) beside the allowlist and before the credential lookup. `egress-proxy/server.mjs` reads `NAMZU_EGRESS_PROXY_CONFIG_V2` (V1 plus `hostPorts`, unknown fields refused), sent instead of V1 only when a profile has ports; `egress-proxy/Dockerfile` gains `LABEL ai.namzu.egress-proxy.config="2"`, which the backend checks with `docker image inspect` before starting such a proxy. `.changeset/sandbox-egress-proxy-ports.md`, **minor**.
- **Creation** [Sandbox egress profiles](sdk/sandbox-egress-profiles.md), draft, for `packages/sandbox/src/egress/profile.ts` and `profile-wiring.ts`: `SandboxEgressProfile` (a DNS-1123 name and hosts with optional ports), `defineEgressProfile`, `egressProfileAllowsPort` (the union over matching rules), `kubernetesEgressFromProfile` and `SandboxEgressProfileError`. `SandboxProviderConfigBase.egressProfile` is new; the page tabulates what each backend makes of it and refuses at construction. With no profile every backend receives what it did before. [Kubernetes sandboxes](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md) gains the translator subsection and [Firecracker sandboxes](sdk/firecracker-sandbox.md) the refusal of ports. `.changeset/sandbox-egress-profiles.md`, **minor**.
- **Update** [Container-tier egress](sdk/sandbox-egress.md): overlapping `setNetworkPolicy()` calls on one docker sandbox now run one at a time, in order, each resolving only once its own proxy container is running (`packages/sandbox/src/backends/docker/index.ts`). Before, the swaps interleaved remove, run, attach and inspect against one container name, and could leave no proxy running while a caller was told its policy was in force. A call equal to the policy in force issues no docker call. `.changeset/sandbox-docker-network-policy-serialized.md`, **patch**.
- **Update** [Session log](sdk/session-log.md): `turn_completed` gains `resultSpill` and `message_replaced` gains `spill`, so a settled answer or a replacement larger than a record spills to `tool-results/` under its own record id instead of being refused; the fold reads a spilled replacement back.
- **Update** [Session log](sdk/session-log.md), four schema corrections in `packages/sdk/src/types/session/records.ts`. `turn_completed` now refuses a `settlement.status` other than `completed` or `cancelled`, and `turn_failed` one other than `failed`, as the page already stated; both used to accept any of the six execution statuses. `SessionRecordSchema` holds a `message` record's `role` equal to `content.role`, `checkpoint_written.throughSeq` below the record's own `seq`, and a `compaction`'s `replacesSeqRange` ascending and before the record. The compaction fixture's manual compaction pointed past itself (`[2, 17]` at seq 14) and now replaces `[2, 13]`.
- **Update** [Session log](sdk/session-log.md), three schema corrections in `packages/sdk/src/types/session/records.ts`. The `audit` record now holds what `AuditEvent` records today, `persona`, `tool`, `resource`, `traceId` and `spanId`, with `outcome` narrowed to `success`, `failure` or `refused`; before, a denied tool call's tool name and the trace link were refused as unknown keys. `child_session_messaged`, `child_session_idled` and `child_session_ended` no longer require `turnId`, so a child that outlives its spawning turn is recorded without naming a closed or unrelated turn. The TypeScript view gives every turn-bound record a non-optional `turnId`, as the schema already guaranteed, and exports the list as `TurnBoundSessionEventType`.
- **Creation** [Session log](sdk/session-log.md), draft: the session → turn → message model's one append-only, hash-chained JSONL file per session. It states the record envelope and chain rules, every record type (the 58 persisted live events and the 14 record-only types), the one-active-turn rule and `TurnInProgressError`, the fold with `message_replaced`, the documents beside the log, and the `NAMZU_HOME` layout with its slug, project and temporary-root rules. The schema is new, additive SDK code in `packages/sdk/src/types/session/{records,events,turn,checkpoint}.ts`, `session/{home,paths,log-hash}.ts` and `utils/uuidv7.ts`; the writer, reader and index that implement it land later, and the run layout is unchanged until then.
- **Update** [Ids](sdk/ids.md): every `generate*Id` factory now mints UUID version 7 (`packages/sdk/src/utils/uuidv7.ts`), monotonic within one process. New `TurnId`/`RecordId` with `generateTurnId`, `generateRecordId`, `asTurnId`, `asRecordId`, and `fixtureId.turn`/`fixtureId.record`.
- **Update** [Request-only step context](sdk/step-context.md), which now also carries the working-memory slot and the new `context` prompt placement, and gains a placements table. Anthropic renders tools, then system, then messages, and both of these used to ride system messages: every pin, and every new send's repository snapshot in the CLI, made the Anthropic driver re-read the whole history, keeping only the tools and static system prompt from cache. Both are now `step-context` messages after the history, before which the driver ends its cache breakpoint. The page also says what a host observing requests sees differently. `turn`, step `system` guidance and the approval-policy notice keep system authority and are unchanged.
- **Update** [Request-only step context](sdk/step-context.md), with a per-driver table of where the history cache breakpoint goes. Bedrock put its message `cachePoint` after the last message, and OpenRouter and Zen's Messages protocol sent a top-level `cache_control` that the upstream applies to the last block; with the working-memory slot now at the tail, each of them cached a prefix no later request repeated. All three now end the breakpoint before the first `step-context` message — OpenRouter and Zen on the last non-system message there, passing over the step preamble, a system message whose text changes per step — and Bedrock merges consecutive same-role messages, which Converse requires. The page also says the closing directive on a forced-final or empty-completion request now comes after all request-only context, and that the working-memory message carries the runtime-generated label.
- **Update** [Zen and Zen Go](sdk/zen.md): Messages-protocol caching places block-level breakpoints instead of a request-level `cache_control`.
- **Update** [Pinned facts](sdk/pinned-facts.md), with a section saying where the slot sits: in the leading system run of the run's history, and after the history in each request.
- **Update** [Resident step context](sdk/resident-context.md), whose note on `turn` now names `context` as the placement for observations that should leave the cached prefix intact.
- **Update** [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md) and [Token budgets](sdk/token-budgets.md), for `resolveRunStorage` in `packages/sdk/src/runtime/query/stores-held-in-memory.ts`. Three things contradicted the page. One `InMemoryRunStore` reused across runs kept one checkpoint store and one ledger for good, holding every run's checkpoints: a probe over 40 runs grew the heap by +9.8 MB. With an explicit `InMemoryCheckpointStore`, a run still wrote `token-budget.json` under `defaultStateRoot()`. A delegated child of an in-memory parent had no run store and wrote its evidence, checkpoints and history there (23 files for one supervisor and one child). Now the ledger lives where the checkpoints live (`InMemoryCheckpointStore.tokenBudgets`). What a run store holds is released when the store is used for another run id and is bounded by `pruneKeepLast` within a run (+3.5 MB after 40 runs, +3.7 MB after 100). `BaseAgentConfig.runStore` and `checkpointStore` reach `query()`, and `AgentTaskContext.childStorage` takes the parent's choice to its children (0 files). Tests that simulated a restart by copying checkpoints now copy the ledger too. `.changeset/in-memory-run-keeps-its-state.md`, **major**.
- **Update** [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md), the run catalogue. States the one forward narrowing of `RunDiskStore.listRuns`: a run started on this version has no `index.json` row, so if its `run.json` is later missing or damaged the listing skips it. Also stated in `.changeset/run-history-log.md`.
- **Update** [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md) and [Token budgets](sdk/token-budgets.md), for `packages/sdk/src/runtime/query/stores-held-in-memory.ts`. A `query()` with an `InMemoryRunStore` and no `pathBuilder` kept its evidence in memory and still wrote `token-budget.json`, its checkpoints and their history log under `defaultStateRoot()`, one tree per run with no retention; the packed `@namzu/live` fixture in `.github/scripts/verify-consumer-install.sh` left one in `~/.local/state/namzu` per gate run. Those now follow the run store: they are held in memory by that run store instance (the new exported `InMemoryTokenBudgetStore`, and `InMemoryCheckpointStore`). A host that passes its own `checkpointStore` keeps the disk ledger, because a resume from those checkpoints with a fresh run store needs it (`latest-user-message-survives-compaction.test.ts` and two others resume that way). The gate points `XDG_STATE_HOME` and `NAMZU_STATE_DIR` into its scratch directory and runs the live fixture under each, requiring both empty. `.changeset/in-memory-run-keeps-its-state.md`, **major** for `@namzu/sdk`: a changed default.
- **Update** [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md), collection. `compactRunHistoryLocked` fsynced the new generation's files and nothing else, then repointed the records through an unsynced rename and unlinked the old generations, so the page's "a crash at any step" held for a process crash and not for a power loss. The history directory is now fsynced after the new generation is named, and each record is rewritten through the new `durableWriteFile` (`packages/sdk/src/utils/atomic-write.ts`, fsync of file then directory) before any unlink. The page states what is and is not guaranteed, Windows included. Folded into `.changeset/run-history-log.md`.
- **Update** [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md), the run catalogue. The deprecated `RunDiskStore.listRuns` read only UUID-named run directories with a readable `run.json`, so runs from before the UUID id format and catalogued runs whose `run.json` was missing or damaged dropped out, contrary to the changeset's "same rows". It now also reads an `index.json` an earlier version left, for exactly those runs, with the `run.json` row winning where both exist. Folded into `.changeset/run-history-log.md`.
- **Update** [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md), for `packages/sdk/src/store/run/run-history.ts` (renamed from `checkpoint-history.ts`, which never shipped) and `packages/sdk/src/store/run/disk.ts`. The history log that checkpoints reference was a second copy of the conversation beside `messages.json`: measured with `scripts/benchmarks/cli-state-growth.mjs 3 50`, `messages.json` was 933,290 bytes and the logs 894,770. The log moved to `<runDir>/history/` and is now the run's one message record: `messages.json` is written as `namzu.run-message-snapshot.v2`, a reference into it (1,092 bytes for the same runs), and v1 files still read. The logs are collected: lines no record references are copied out into a new generation once they outweigh the live ones (`compactRunHistoryLocked`), so with retention at 10 a 200-iteration run's log is 314,523 bytes instead of 968,819. `index.json` is no longer written; the deprecated `RunDiskStore.listRuns` reads the same rows from each `run.json`. `report.md` is kept, because `run.json` does not carry `result`. Each checkpoint of a listing now has message objects of its own. `.changeset/run-history-log.md`, **major** for `@namzu/sdk`.
- **Update** [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md) and [Run the kernel](sdk/quick-start.md), for `defaultStateRoot()` (`packages/sdk/src/session/workspace/state-root.ts`). With no `pathBuilder`, `query` (`runtime/query/context.ts`), a resume's budget lookup (`runtime/query/token-budget.ts`) and `openTokenBudget` (`store/run/token-budget-disk.ts`) defaulted to `<workingDirectory>/.namzu`, which is what an eval and any embedder hit, and a run in `$HOME` landed in the CLI's `~/.namzu` with no Project record. The default is now `NAMZU_STATE_DIR`, else the platform's per-user state directory. The SDK test runner, the CLI's test home and every other package's Vitest setup (`tools/vitest-state-root.mjs`) point `NAMZU_STATE_DIR` at a scratch directory. `.changeset/sdk-default-state-root.md`, **major** for `@namzu/sdk`.
- **Update** [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md), retention section. `CheckpointManager.prune` listed every checkpoint, resolving every history against the log, after every iteration, and a throw aborted the run. It now calls the new optional `CheckpointStore.pruneCheckpoints`, which `DiskCheckpointStore` implements from the checkpoint files alone (`selectCheckpointsToPrune`, `store/run/prune.ts`), and a failing prune is logged while the run continues (`runtime/query/iteration/phases/checkpoint.ts`). `.changeset/checkpoint-store-prune.md`, **minor**.
- **Update** [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md) and [Project and session state](cli/project-state.md), crash dumps. The only code path that resumes from a dump, `prepareReplayState({ fromCheckpoint: 'emergency' })`, forks a new run id, so the completion cleanup never reached its dump. It now returns `emergencySavePath`, and `query({ supersedesEmergencySave })` removes that dump when the replay completes. The CLI, which continues a conversation under a new run id and never reads a dump, removes a session's dumps older than a turn when that turn completes (`clearOutlivedEmergencySaves`, `packages/cli/src/integrations/state/retention.ts`). The kernel deleting a dump it used to keep is a changed default, so `.changeset/clear-outlived-crash-dump.md` is now **major**; `.changeset/cli-clear-outlived-dumps.md` is **major** for `@namzu/cli`.
- **Update** [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md), for `packages/evals/kernel/*.eval.js`. Each case created a scratch directory, ran with no path builder and never removed it, leaving a Project tree in the system temporary directory per case. Each case now keeps its state inside the directory and removes it. `.changeset/evals-clean-scratch.md`.
- **Update** [Project and session state](cli/project-state.md), measurements re-taken against `main` at `cf271eb9` on the same machine: 3 × 50 tool calls, 173 files and 29,621,057 bytes before, 53 and 4,101,738 after; 1 × 200, 209 and 81,982,930 before, 20 and 6,283,563 after. `namzu state` no longer classifies history logs as checkpoint files, since they are the run's message record.
- **Update** [Project and session state](cli/project-state.md) gains a runtime-state-growth section. Nothing in `packages/cli` set `runConfig.pruneKeepLast` (`packages/sdk/src/runtime/query/iteration/phases/checkpoint.ts` prunes only when it is set), so every CLI run kept every checkpoint. `CLI_CHECKPOINT_RETENTION = 10` (`packages/cli/src/integrations/state/retention.ts`) now goes to every run the CLI starts: turns (`runTurn`), resumed and drained runs, and delegated children (`subagents/runtime.ts`, through the new `BaseAgentConfig.pruneKeepLast`). The page cites `scripts/benchmarks/cli-state-growth.mjs`, the built `namzu run` against a local scripted endpoint. For 3 invocations of 50 tool calls, the application home goes from 173 files and 29,621,057 bytes to 56 files and 5,034,053 bytes. For 1 invocation of 200, it goes from 209 files and 81,982,921 bytes to 21 files and 7,236,819 bytes. `namzu state` now counts the checkpoint history logs among checkpoint files (`integrations/state/report.ts`). `.changeset/cli-checkpoint-retention.md`, **major** for `@namzu/cli`: a changed default.
- **Update** [Project and session state](cli/project-state.md) and [Memory](cli/memory.md), for `createAgentSession` in `packages/cli/src/tui/agent.ts` and `readMemory` in `packages/cli/src/memory/store.ts`. With no `stateRoot`, a session defaulted its generated state to `<cwd>/.namzu`. Every production entry point passes a state root, so the default fired only for embedders and tests, which is how runtime trees appeared at `packages/cli/.namzu/projects/…` (four `Screens fixture` runs from `a-refusal-reaches-the-transcript.test.ts`, which also ran the kernel with `workingDirectory: process.cwd()`). The default is now the application home (`NAMZU_HOME`, else `~/.namzu`). A scope-less session derives its Project from the checkout (`projectIdForDirectory(cliProjectRoot(cwd))`) instead of minting one per launch. Generated memory is always partitioned by Project, where before it was unpartitioned at `<root>/memory` whenever no state root was given. Started in `$HOME`, the project memory file is the user memory file, and it was injected twice; it is now read once, as the user's. The two test files that wrote runtime state into their package now use scratch directories: `a-refusal-reaches-the-transcript.test.ts` in the CLI and `background-delegation.live.test.ts` in `@namzu/anthropic`. `.changeset/cli-central-generated-state.md`.
- **Update** [Run the kernel](sdk/quick-start.md), [Ids](sdk/ids.md) and [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md), for `projectIdForDirectory` (`packages/sdk/src/utils/id.ts`) and `runAgent`'s changed default. `runAgent` minted a `projectId` per call (`packages/sdk/src/agents/runAgent.ts`), and every durable path is keyed by it, so a batch of runs in one directory left one `projects/<id>/` tree per run. An omitted `projectId` is now a name-based UUID (version 8) over the canonical working directory, the same in every process and for every spelling of the directory. The page states what can and cannot be concluded about the 79 `prj_*` trees measured in one home directory: they have this layout's shape and a pre-UUID build's ids, and the call site that minted them is not recoverable. The current headless CLI already resolves one Project per checkout (measured). `.changeset/run-agent-directory-project.md`, **major** for `@namzu/sdk`: a changed default.
- **Update** [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md) gains a crash-dump section. `EmergencySaveManager.clearSave` had no caller, and replay reads a dump through `loadSave` without ever removing one. The CLI turns dumps on for every interactive turn, so each crash or interrupt left the whole conversation on disk for good. `ResultAssembler.finalize` (`packages/sdk/src/runtime/query/result.ts`) now removes `<runDir>/../emergency/<runId>.json` when the run settles `completed`, which is how a crashed run that was resumed under its own id and finished ends. A failed or paused resume keeps the dump, and a replay forked from a dump runs under a new id and leaves it alone. `EmergencySaveManager.savePathFor` is the single place that names the path, used by the writer and the cleanup alike. `.changeset/clear-outlived-crash-dump.md`, **minor** for `@namzu/sdk`.
- **Creation** [Durable run storage](sdk/run-storage.md), for `packages/sdk/src/store/run/checkpoint-history.ts` and the schema-3 checkpoint in `packages/sdk/src/store/run/disk.ts`. A checkpoint used to carry the whole message history inline, one per iteration plus one per tool review. On the owner's machine that came to 19,014 checkpoint files and 6.33 GB of 7.0 GB of runtime state, with one run holding 532 checkpoints of about 1.5 MB each. Each distinct message is now appended once to one of two per-run logs. `history.jsonl` holds the messages that extend the history. `history-edits.jsonl` holds the ones that replace or are inserted into it: the pin slot rewritten every iteration, a compaction's summary, a cleared tool result. A checkpoint stores `[log, offset, length, count]` ranges plus a SHA-256 over the referenced bytes. Readers still get `messages`, a damaged log is refused just as a damaged inline checkpoint is, schema-1 and schema-2 checkpoints read unchanged, and checkpoint files are written compact. The page also inventories every per-run file, with its writer, readers and whether it is authoritative, and cites `scripts/benchmarks/runtime-state-growth.mjs`. For a 50-iteration run, checkpoints go from 10,206,928 bytes to 1,133,961 bytes of records plus 183,939 bytes of logs; for 200 iterations, from 108,807,126 to 6,674,382 plus 968,424. `BaseAgentConfig.pruneKeepLast` is new and forwarded by `ReactiveAgent` and `SupervisorAgent`, so a host can bound a delegated child's checkpoints as it bounds its own. `.changeset/checkpoint-history-log.md` is **major** for `@namzu/sdk`, because an older SDK refuses a schema-3 checkpoint. `.changeset/agent-prune-keep-last.md` is **minor**.
- **Update** [Memory](cli/memory.md) and [Structured memory](sdk/memory.md#markdown-memory-files), for a third round of typed-memory findings. (1) `/memory import-notes` guessed which bullets were a hand-written list: a blank line reset its context, so a heading followed by a blank line (the usual Markdown style) had its whole section offered and moved, leaving the heading empty. It is now non-destructive: it copies every top-level bullet into a typed memory and never writes the curated file, and the heading/blank-line rules, the `MEMORY.md.before-typed-memory` backups and the moved count are gone; a second import creates no duplicates (a bullet whose digest, `#note` digest or same-named same-text memory is already stored, archived included, is skipped), and the report says the file is unchanged and deleting bullets is the operator's to do. (2) The previous round refused every pair of files claiming one id when one was undated, so renaming a hand-written memory and crashing before the old file was unlinked failed every `list`, `create` and `readIndex` — and cost the CLI the stored-memory index every turn. A pair of an undated file whose id is derived from its own name and a dated file stating that id is again resolved by the newer (the undated side by mtime); an undated file that states its id is still refused with both files named. (3) `/memory show|list` left out what the run promoter and consolidation wrote, showing nothing when only those existed; they now appear in their own `Recorded by runs (N)` section, still outside the prompt's index, via the new `readIndex({ derived: true })` / `renderMemoryIndex(records, { derived: true })`. Regressions: `typed.test.ts`, `provider-boundary.test.ts`, `presentation.test.ts`, `app-picker-exits.test.tsx`, `markdown-store.test.ts`.
- **Fix** a second round of review findings in typed memory, documented in [Structured memory](sdk/memory.md#markdown-memory-files) and [Memory](cli/memory.md). (1) The CLI's run promoter writes into the `MarkdownMemoryStore` whose index is in every system prompt, and `create` names every record, so nearly every run added an index line: the system prompt changed almost every turn, invalidating the prompt cache from there on, and the name-sorted 200-line cap dropped operator memories that sorted late. The index now leaves out records the runtime derived (`metadata.source: 'run-memory'`, `metadata.kind: 'consolidation'`; `store/memory/origin.ts`), which stay searchable, and orders operator `feedback`/`user` memories first, then the model's, then the rest, by name within each; `renderMemoryIndex` takes `MemoryRecord`s to see that metadata. (2) `/memory import-notes` never reset at a blank line, so `# Project memory`, a blank line, then `#note` bullets were kept as a heading's list and never offered; a blank line now ends a heading's list, and the docs and the report say exactly what stays. (3) Two files with one id and different `updatedAt` were taken for an interrupted rename even when one had no `updatedAt` of its own (a hand copy, dated by mtime), and the older was set aside silently; such a pair is now refused. (4) A body ending in `\r` lost it on a round trip; the writer closes such a body with `\r\n`. (5) A NUL in a title, summary, description or tag reached `MEMORY.md` and the prompt; the store refuses it before writing and the loader refuses a file that spells one. (6) A second `/memory import-notes` kept the first run's backup and claimed it as the file before the move, and the marker recorded bullets found rather than memories created; each run now keeps its own text (`-2`, `-3`) and the marker counts creations. (7) A stale comment in `cli/src/memory/store.ts` said launches move notes. Regressions: `markdown-store.test.ts`, `typed.test.ts`, `a-promoter-reaches-the-turn.test.ts` (a promoter write leaves the next turn's `systemPrompt` byte-identical).
- **Fix** review findings in typed memory, documented in [Structured memory](sdk/memory.md#markdown-memory-files) and [Memory](cli/memory.md). (1) `MarkdownMemoryStore` wrote files its own loader refused — a body over 256 KiB or with a NUL byte — after which every operation in the directory failed, and a legacy JSON record over the limit stopped the migration with `index.json` in place, so the store refused everything on every launch; the store now refuses such content with `MemoryContentRejectedError` before writing, `save_memory`/`update_memory` return it as a failed result, and the migration skips and names the record, keeping it in `content.migrated/`. (2) `update_memory` required a UUID while the prompt's index shows only names; it now takes a name, like `read_memory`. (3) An index line spent most of its 150 characters on a derived slug written twice, and a 64-character name broke the limit (`Math.max(room, 16)`); derived names are at most 32 characters and the line is hard-capped. (4) The launch moved every single-line curated bullet, hand-written ones included, emptying operator sections; it now offers them once per curated file and `/memory import-notes` moves them on request, leaving a heading's list. (5) The migration marker ignored which curated file it recorded; it is now per file. (6) `read_memory`'s footer made a JSON body unparseable; JSON bodies are returned exactly, and the footer's output change has its own **major** changeset (`.changeset/read-memory-age-footer.md`). (7) `description: [WIP] notes` and a crash mid-rename each disabled the store; the first reads as a string, the second resolves by the later `updatedAt`, setting the older file aside. (8) `.changeset/consolidation-digest-dedup.md` is reclassified **major**: a documented event and write no longer happen for some runs. Regressions: `markdown-store.test.ts`, `typed-memory-tools.test.ts`, `typed.test.ts`, `provider-boundary.test.ts`.
- **Update** [Memory](cli/memory.md), for the CLI's move to typed stored memory. The CLI had two systems both called memory, and its own comments flagged it (`cli/src/memory/store.ts`, `tui/agent.ts`): curated files whose `#note` bullets accumulated unstructured under an 8,000-character cap and were re-read whole every turn, and a JSON store the model searched but the operator could not read. Stored memory is now a `MarkdownMemoryStore` at the store's existing root (the directory stays injectable through `projectStateRoot`), so the files `save_memory` writes are the files an operator can open. `#note` and `/memory add` write typed files through it (default `project`, `--type` for another; an identical active note is reported rather than saved twice); `/memory --user add` still appends to the user-scope curated file, since stored memory belongs to one project. Every send and resume carries the generated index under `## Stored memories (index)`, capped at 200 lines, and the curated sections become `## Curated memory (all projects)` and `## Curated memory (this project)` so the two are never both called memory of the same kind; an unreadable memory file costs that turn its index and shows a notice, not the turn. First launch migrates the JSON store's records once and idempotently, with their ids (then `index.json.migrated`); the project curated file's single-line bullets are offered and move only on `/memory import-notes` (see the entry above). Regression: `provider-boundary.test.ts` drives a real session through a scripted provider and asserts the migrated note, both headings and the absence of "Durable memory" in the outgoing system prompt; `typed.test.ts` covers the bullet split, duplicate notes and a rerun that imports nothing. `.changeset/cli-typed-stored-memory.md`, **major** for `@namzu/cli`: the destination of `#note` and `/memory add` changes — the same class as the release that moved `#note` to the project file, which shipped as major — and two prompt headings are renamed; the changeset says how to keep writing curated notes.
- **Fix** consolidation's missing deduplication, documented in [Structured memory](sdk/memory.md#promotion-after-a-run). `createMemoryPromoter` skipped a record whose claim digest the store already held, archived included; `consolidateInto`, the other writer into the same store, had no such check, so every run that settled with the same decisions, discoveries and failures wrote the same learning again and the store's search results filled with copies. `consolidationEntry` now carries a `knowledge:<digest>` tag and `metadata.knowledgeDigest` over what was learned (not the run id or the task), plus `type: 'project'`, and the runtime asks the new `isConsolidated(store, entry)` before writing, skipping the write and its `memory_consolidated` event on a match. Both writers share one lookup (`store/memory/digest.ts`), which tags narrow and metadata confirms. Regression: `a-run-writes-down-what-it-learned.test.ts` runs the same failing run three times against one store — the second and third write nothing, the third after the first record was archived. `.changeset/consolidation-digest-dedup.md`, **major** for `@namzu/sdk` (reclassified, see the entry above): a host that counted one consolidation per run is told in the changeset how to find the earlier record.
- **Update** [Structured memory](sdk/memory.md), for typed memory and a store that keeps one Markdown file per memory. Two things were both called memory and neither had a type: the CLI's curated files, and the `MemoryStore` records whose `MemoryIndexEntry` carried `title`, `summary`, `tags` and `status` and nothing saying what kind of claim a record was or what it was for. `MemoryIndexEntry`, `CreateMemoryParams` and `UpdateMemoryParams` gain optional `name` (a unique kebab slug), `description` (one line) and `type` (`user`, `feedback`, `project`, `reference`); every shipped store persists them, search scores them as the title and summary they stand beside, and an explicit name another record holds is refused with `MemoryNameConflictError`, which names the holder so the caller updates it. `MarkdownMemoryStore` implements the same contract as `DiskMemoryStore` — operation lock (the same `operation.lock`, so the two never interleave on one directory), reload per operation, atomic 0600 writes, refusal naming the file — with each memory as `<name>.md` (frontmatter plus body, body round-tripped byte for byte) and a generated `MEMORY.md` index, one line per active memory, clipped to 150 characters; `readIndex` renders it for a prompt capped at 200 lines with a note pointing to search, and `importRecord` moves records in idempotently by id — the one operation allowed while a `DiskMemoryStore` `index.json` still sits in the directory, since answering without that index's records would present a smaller memory as the whole. The frontmatter reader is its own, because `utils/frontmatter.ts` refuses lists and mappings by design and a memory has tags and metadata; it reads plain, JSON and single-quoted values and block lists and refuses everything else, unknown keys included. `read_memory` takes a name, reports age and resolves `[[name]]` links; recall adds `name`, `type`, and an `age` plus a verify-before-relying notice for records older than a day. `DiskMemoryStore` stopped handing out its files' `schemaVersion` stamp inside `MemoryContent`. `.changeset/typed-markdown-memory.md`, **minor** for `@namzu/sdk`: every change is an added optional field, export, tool parameter or recall field; nothing is removed, renamed or narrowed, and a caller that passes none of the new fields gets the old behaviour.

## 2026-09-19

- **Update** `packages/providers/zen/src/models.ts`, documented in [Zen and Zen Go](sdk/zen.md), whose catalogue loses one effort level from one model: `muse-spark-1.3` no longer advertises `max`, because upstream dropped it from the model's own menu and the generator writes what upstream states. A caller passing `effort: 'max'` for that model now meets the refusal the driver has always given for an unadvertised level — “The selected model does not advertise this reasoning effort level.” No code changed and no id left the roster, so the model still resolves with the same protocol, prices, context window and tool support; the public `ReasoningEffort` union still carries `max` for the models that advertise it. Bump `minor` on the catalogue's own stated rule — a removal is `major` because a carried id stops resolving, and this one does not, which makes it the class that page calls a repricing. `node scripts/generate-zen-models.mjs` regenerated it; `models.ts` is never hand-edited.

- **Fix** `packages/sandbox/agent/agent.cjs`'s terminal slave discovery for a guest whose kernel was built without `CONFIG_PROC_CHILDREN` (#516, a follow-up to #512). The walk that finds the shell util-linux `script` forked had exactly one way in — `processChildren`, reading `/proc/<pid>/task/<pid>/children` — and that file is not part of the kernel's base `/proc` support but an option, so a guest built without it has no such entry at all. The reporter listed `/proc/<pid>/task/<pid>/` and `children` is simply absent, where it would sort between `cgroup` and `clear_refs`, for a `script` that was alive with a shell under it: `cat /proc/108/task/108/children` → `No such file or directory`. Every read therefore answered `ENOENT`, the queue was `[]` on all 200 attempts, `probePtySlave` was never called, and the `tty_nr` fallback #512 shipped — which is per pid the walk has already DEQUEUED — was unreachable, because it never dequeued one. Their report reads the consequence out loud: `200 attempts, 1112ms; last children(): ENOENT; last fd/0: never read; last stat: never read; script: alive`. `parseProcessStat` now carries field 4 as well, `ppid`, and one pass over `/proc` groups every live pid by its parent — `scanChildrenByParent` — which is the DIRECT-children relation the walk needs, from the same kernel, through a file that is world-readable and gated on nothing. It is NOT the file's relation exactly, and the code says so rather than calling the two the same: field 4 is the parent's **thread-group id**, so the map is the thread-group-collapsed graph — WIDER than the file at a leader node, where a child forked by any thread of the group is listed under the leader while the file's `children` names the leader's own children only, and EMPTY at a non-leader tid, a node `readdir('/proc')` never yields and the walk never asks about. Neither difference takes a candidate away from the walk: the file's listing at a leader is a subset of the scan's, every node this walk visits is a process rather than a thread, and what the scan can add is a child the file's own walk would have missed (verified here rather than reasoned about — a `sleep` forked by a worker thread of a probe process reported its parent's tgid, appeared in the worker tid's `children` file alone, and `readdir('/proc')` listed no non-leader tid at all). `findPtySlave` reads the file first and always and reaches the scan only through `discoverChildren`, which is where the ambiguity is settled: `ENOENT` means the kernel RELEASED this pid when `/proc/<pid>` is gone and the kernel has no such file when `/proc/<pid>` is still there, told apart by the `procEntryState` the file already had; a pid that is gone ends the walk on the same observation it always did, and a read that was REFUSED (`EACCES`, `EMFILE`) takes the path it took before — no scan, errno reported as it was. The scan is DISCOVERY only and never acceptance: `probePtySlave` is still the only thing that can accept a candidate, so the `fd/0`-then-`tty_nr` order, the pid that comes back with the slave (the shell's — `ownedSessionIds` and the teardown rest on it), the breadth-first order and the early stop are all unchanged, and it answers DIRECT children — with those two differences and no candidate lost to either — which is what lets the walk recurse through it the way it recurses through the file. Cost was decided rather than inherited, on two axes. One pass per ATTEMPT, built lazily and shared by every pid that attempt visits — one `/proc` pass per 5ms poll for K candidates rather than K passes — because the thing being waited for is the fork the tree has not made yet, so a scan taken once per walk would answer about a tree that has since grown, while a scan per dequeued pid would make a guest that needs it pay for a pass per candidate; and on any kernel WITH the file, which is every guest this agent has been measured in, the scan never runs at all. And the pass itself reads each pid's `stat` with `readFileSync`, the one synchronous reader in the agent, chosen by measurement: ~12µs per pid against ~276µs for the promisified read (56-process `/proc`, WSL), so a pass is 0.7ms rather than 15ms — syscall time alone, since procfs builds that line in kernel memory and there is no device behind it to block on, whereas the async form pays an event-loop round trip per pid, P times a pass, 200 passes a walk. That cost is LINEAR in the machine's process count and there is no small-P ceiling to assume from the 0.7ms: driving the real function — extracted from the shipping file — over a `/proc` holding a copy of `sleep` per spawned process measured 0.58ms at 42 live pids, 11ms at 1041 and 45ms at 4041. The listing that precedes the loop stays asynchronous, being one call per pass rather than one per pid. And the stall is BOUNDED rather than merely measured, because this agent is single-threaded and serves every terminal, exec, stream and `healthz` on that one loop, while on a kernel without the option the scan runs on EVERY attempt: the loop hands the loop back every 64 reads (a `setImmediate`), which caps the uninterrupted stretch at 64 reads and not at the machine's size. Measured: the longest gap between two yields was 0.97-1.05ms at 4041 live pids and 0.69-0.93ms at 1041, where the same passes with the yield removed held the loop for 40.1-42.9ms and 10.6-11.8ms. The turns are what the bound costs — 62 of them, 41.7ms → 45.1ms at 4041 pids (median of five) — and below 64 reads no yield fires at all, the pass being shorter than the stretch it is allowed. Where it does run, the failure message says so — `last children(): ENOENT, discovered by a /proc scan` — because `ENOENT` alone reads as a broken walk rather than as a kernel that has no such file, and that misreading is what cost the reporter a cluster to correct — while a scan whose own `readdir('/proc')` FAILED reports that instead (`last children(): ENOENT, and the /proc scan that stands in for it could not list /proc: EACCES`), because a listing that never happened must not be answered with a scan that ran and came back empty, which is the same misreading one level down; the leading phrase, every other field and `script`'s own three-valued line are untouched. **The absence cannot be produced here** — this machine's kernel has the option and the reporter's guest is a cluster — so the regression test arranges it the way this suite already arranges what it cannot reproduce: `packages/sandbox/src/backends/kubernetes/__tests__/terminal-pty-slave.test.ts` writes a COPY of the shipped agent whose one `children` read is pointed at a path that is not there, by an anchored edit that asserts the anchor occurs exactly once (a rename fails loudly rather than turning the patch into a no-op), and drives it over the same loopback socket and real processes as every other case there. Against the pre-fix source exactly those two discovery cases fail, and with the reporter's own line: `terminal PTY slave did not appear (200 attempts, 1043ms; last children(): ENOENT; last fd/0: never read; last stat: never read; script: alive)`; against this one they answer `ready` in milliseconds, resize the shell's real pty (read back out of the line discipline with `stty -F`) and still end a SIGHUP-immune background job through the shell's own kernel session — so the cases pin the DISCOVERY and the two contracts that must not move with it. The third new case pins the COST instead of the defect: an instrumented `/proc`, asserting that a walk which can read the file never runs the scan at all — and the count is attributed to the scan's own stack frame rather than to the path, because the teardown's `/proc` scan is an unawaited reader that a FAILED case leaves in flight and it lands inside the next case's window (measured: a plain path-count fails the bound case on that stray). That count is a counter and not a constant, and the file proves it from both sides — the two cases that must scan assert it saw theirs, so renaming the scan fails them loudly instead of turning the bound into a vacuous pass (verified by renaming it). The bound case passes against the pre-fix source too, which has no scan to run; what it holds is the decision to reach the fallback only from an absent file, against an implementation that scans unconditionally (verified against one). **Stays unproven**: that this agent answers `ready` in the reporter's guest. Not pinned by a test, and deliberately: a timing assertion on the yield would be flaky, and the suite has no seam for a denied `/proc` listing. Both are verified out of suite instead — the stall table above by measurement, the failed-listing message by driving `discoverChildren` with a faked `fs` — which is why those numbers are quoted here rather than asserted in the file. The kernel condition is arranged, never reproduced, and confirming it means rerunning the reporter's own repro against an image rebuilt from this commit plus the two reads that name the condition: `cat /proc/$$/task/$$/children` (`No such file or directory` if the option is off) and the `ppid` scan that stands in for it, which must name the same child `/proc` gives this machine. `.changeset/children-file-absent.md`, **patch** for `@namzu/sandbox`: `agent.cjs` is a guest/deployment file baked into the guest image by `k8s/Dockerfile` and absent from the published tarball (`npm pack @namzu/sandbox@18.1.0 --dry-run` lists 0 entries for it), and no public type, export, wire shape or documented default changed. No page in `docs/` describes how the slave is found — [Kubernetes sandboxes](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md#a-terminal-or-a-program-can-outlive-the-host-process) documents the teardown contract that is preserved, not altered — so the log is the only doc that changes.
- **Update** [Zen and Zen Go](sdk/zen.md), for the second route-row shape the Zen page publishes and the first one the generator refused to read. The page routes `jev-1.13` and `jev-1.13-free` on an endpoint whose AI SDK package column carries the dash its own tables give a cell with no value, and `parsePage` matched a route row only when BOTH halves were backticked — so the row fell through to the shape guard, which counts cells and code spans, and the run stopped at exit 2 with `ZEN CATALOGUE SOURCE UNUSABLE`. That guard was right to fire and wrong about what it had found: the row is not a page that lost a column, it is a page saying there is no wire, and a model the page routes could not enter the machinery at all, so no curation decision about it could even be made. A fourth cell holding exactly the page's own `-` is now read as a route row that states no wire, `derive` reports it through the `undecided` bucket that was already there for decisions — before the row is put to models.dev, so `considered` still means the rows actually asked about and a row no answer could make carryable is not one of them — and the guard keeps its teeth around the new shape: an empty package cell, a dropped package column and a marker the page does not use (`n/a`) all still land in `unroutable` and still stop the run. That difference is the whole of it: reading a statement the page makes is not the same edit as turning the check off. Both ids are omitted in `src/models.review.json`, in the file's voice, with the fact that makes them uncarryable — the page states no package for them, and models.dev holds a name and limits for both and no package either. Checked against the service rather than assumed, because it shapes the wording: the Zen `/models` answer serves both ids, so they are documented AND served and still not routable, and the omissions are what stand between them and a driver that would never offer them. With the page readable again the gate reported what it had been unable to reach before: upstream had added `qwen3.8-flash` (Messages, $0.15/$0.47) and `deepseek-v4.1-flash` (Chat Completions, $0.30/$1.20) to the Zen route table. That drift is independent of this change and was measured as such — the unmodified script reports it against a page with every trace of those two rows deleted — both route rows, both price rows and the free-list line that names the free one, because deleting the route rows alone leaves that line naming a model the route table no longer has and the unmodified script stops there instead — so the roster is refreshed with it, 59 Zen and 27 Go becomes 61 and 27, and the release is `minor`, the bump this catalogue uses for an addition. `scripts/__tests__/generate-zen-models.test.ts` pins both halves of the new shape, the row that is read and the reason it is reported, and its neighbour pins the marker that is still a source failure.
- **Fix** `packages/sandbox/agent/agent.cjs`'s terminal slave discovery (#512): a Kubernetes `openTerminal` could fail with `terminal PTY slave did not appear` even though `script` had forked its shell and the PTY was there. `findPtySlave` walked `/proc` for the shell under `script` and accepted the first candidate whose `readlink('/proc/<pid>/fd/0')` matched `/dev/pts/<n>` — the one probe it had, and one the kernel answers through `ptrace_may_access`, which refuses a reader whose credentials do not match the target's (`/proc/<pid>/fd` is `dr-x------` owned by the target, while `/proc/<pid>/stat` is world readable). The bare `catch` around it turned that refusal into "keep looking", so the loop spent its whole one-second budget on a live tree and then threw a message that named nothing, which is why this needed a cluster to find. Measured in a real kind cluster: with the reader's uid differing from the target's, `fd/0` read as `<EACCES>` for every attempt while `/proc/<pid>/stat` read fine and named `major=136 minor=3 -> /dev/pts/3` — the same slave root sees through `fd/0` for that live process — and the pre-fix agent answered `{"type":"error","error":"terminal PTY slave did not appear"}` in 1.28s where the fixed one answers `ready` in 7ms, resizes that same `/dev/pts/3` to 40x100 (read back out of the pty's line discipline with `stty -F`), and still ends a SIGHUP-immune background job through the shell's kernel session on teardown. `readProcessStat` now carries field 7 as well — `tty_nr`, decoded with the kernel's own `new_decode_dev`, major 136 being devpts — and `findPtySlave` asks `fd/0` FIRST, falling back to the controlling terminal only when the readlink fails or answers with something that is not a PTY slave; because the walk still returns the first candidate in breadth-first order, the pid it reports is the one the fd 0 probe alone returned whenever that probe can answer at all, which is what `ownedSessionIds = [child.pid, shellSessionId]` and the session teardown rest on. It also stops as soon as `/proc/<script>` is gone instead of spending the rest of the budget on a released pid — and a terminal whose `script` has already exited is answered with its own `exit` frame rather than with this error, because the walk gives up on the same fact that ends the terminal and a walk error there would be a claim about a terminal that had already ended, with the exit code lost. The failure now names what it could not read, and names only what it read (`terminal PTY slave did not appear (200 attempts, 1002ms; last children(): EACCES; last fd/0: EACCES; last stat: EACCES; script: alive)`): every field is the LAST OBSERVATION of the probe it names rather than the last error that probe ever had, and `script`'s own line is a third value — `alive`, `gone`, or the errno that stopped it being read — where a read that never happened used to be reported as `script alive`. The leading phrase is unchanged, so anything matching on it already matches. Regression test: `packages/sandbox/src/backends/kubernetes/__tests__/terminal-pty-slave.test.ts` — six cases, four of which fail against the pre-fix source and pass against this one (one of those four only most of the time — 4, 4, 4, 3, 4 failures over five full-file runs: the case's denial is a setgid copy of `/bin/sh`, and the pre-fix walk's probe can land on the intermediate shell `script -c` runs to execute that command line, still non-setgid and still holding the PTY on fd 0, in the millisecond before it execs that copy — answering `ready` for the same pid the test later reads as `<EACCES>` and calls the shell); the strongest is a real kernel denial, a setgid copy of `/bin/sh` whose egid the reading process is not in, with the denial asserted before anything is asserted about the agent, standing down on a machine that cannot produce it. The two that drive the FAILING walk pin its teardown as well as its message — the tree the walk can reach goes, which is the contract that had no assertion at all, and a reap in `afterEach` makes that the test's own cleanup rather than a promise the agent's deliberately unawaited kill may not keep. What that path cannot reach is stated there rather than closed: it knows `script`'s process group and nothing else, the shell's kernel session being the thing it failed to find, so a job that ignores SIGHUP outlives the failure — asserted, not merely described — and the sixth case pins the `exit` frame (and the exit code) a terminal is answered with when its program ends before the slave is found. **What is not proven**: which condition in the reporter's cluster made the readlink fail for the whole budget — the uid-mismatch shape reproduced here looks impossible in the shipped image, where agent, `script` and shell are all uid 1001, and no run of the unmodified agent in a kind cluster reproduced the failure. This commit removes the dependency and makes the failure legible; it does not claim the trigger. `.changeset/terminal-pty-slave-without-fd-0.md`, **patch** for `@namzu/sandbox`: `agent.cjs` is a guest/deployment file outside the published tarball and no public type, export, wire shape or documented default changed. No page in `docs/` describes how the slave is found — [Kubernetes sandboxes](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md#the-sandbox-surface) documents `openTerminal`'s teardown contract, which is preserved, not altered — so the log is the only doc that changes.
- **Update** [Container-tier egress](sdk/sandbox-egress.md), whose "what the boundary does not cover" list is one entry shorter because the gap it recorded is closed. `createSandboxProvider` never forwarded `brokeredCredentials`: the field is declared on `DockerBackendInternalConfig`, read by `egressProxyContainerConfig`, which puts it into the proxy container's environment — and the provider passed `allowInwardFor` two lines away at BOTH of its `buildDockerBackend` call sites and dropped this one. So the credential brokering the whole internal-network topology exists to provide was reachable only by constructing the docker backend directly, which is not the documented way to build a provider, and a host that did pass it through a cast got a proxy that forwarded every request unauthenticated rather than a refusal. The field is now on `ContainerBackendConfig`, declared on the tier that has a proxy to stamp at: the microVM tier hands its allowlist to its guest orchestrator and the kubernetes tier writes `NetworkPolicy`/`CiliumNetworkPolicy`, neither has a proxy, and a knob on either could never be honoured — the silently-ignored option this package refuses everywhere else, and the one place a type could still express it. The JSDoc and the page also state the narrower case inside the tier: `deny-all` and `allow-all` start no proxy, so credentials set beside them are never applied. Both call sites forward it in the guarded style the file uses, so an absent field stays absent rather than becoming an explicit `undefined`. `BrokeredCredential` remains a type-only export from the package entry point; it gained a local type import beside that re-export, because a re-export introduces no local name and the config field needs one to point at. Three cases were added to `src/egress/__tests__/exemption-reaches-the-backend.test.ts` — carries them, leaves them absent when none were brokered, and carries them on the `runsc` runtime too — and two of the three failed against the previous sources, the third asserting an absence the previous sources satisfied by setting nothing at all. Bump `minor`: one additive optional field, no default changed, nothing removed, and a config that does not set it starts exactly the proxy container it started before.

- **Update** [Provider credentials and private state](cli/credentials.md), for the rule the picker's sub-menu was gated on. It asked "which provider did you mean" only when a row's ways in came from more than one registry id, which answered the wrong question in both directions: `anthropic` is ONE id that takes a signed-in session and an API key, so a machine where discovery found that session had two real choices and was asked nothing, while two ids that were the same key would have been asked twice. The gate is the number of WAYS now — his words for it are "show it if it exists, hide it if it does not" — and the key path is offered beside what discovery found whenever what was found is not itself a key. That last part is asked with `signedInSubscriptionProviders`, the predicate the sign-in screen already decides it by, so an OAuth token in an environment variable counts as the session it is on both screens rather than as a key here. What follows each answer is unchanged and shared: the session, the free catalogue and any other detected way lead to the models, the key leads to the paste field and then the models, and a row with ONE way in keeps the keystroke it always had — an exported OpenRouter or DeepSeek key, a local server, and an unconfigured vendor all go straight on. Verified in a PTY at 100x30: the Anthropic row now offers two ways — the session discovery found on this device, and the key it can take — with the first leading to its four models and the second to the paste field; Zen offers its free catalogue beside the key that catalogue does not need; OpenAI is unchanged; and OpenRouter still lands on its 446-model list with no sub-menu at all.
- **Update** [Provider credentials and private state](cli/credentials.md), for four defects a PTY run found on the picker which a rendering test at 100 columns could not. Every one of them was in a width or a height the tests never used. The screen's text area was computed as `columns - 4` — the border and the padding — and the app's own one-column inset on each side was forgotten, so at 60 columns a row was measured against 56 and drawn in 54: the note beside the model the picker had just marked `(current)` wrapped onto a line of its own, which is the row the cursor is on. It is now derived from the three terms that take the columns, and the composition is pinned by a test that renders the picker inside the app's inset and reads the width off the border the box drew. The cursor row lost the space between its marker and its number (`›6. DeepSeek`), because a row that is wider than the box is squeezed and the squeeze takes the trailing space of the text nodes the row is built from; the row's segments are children of one text node now, and the source column is wrapped by the screen so the row stays inside the area at all. On a 60x20 terminal the box was taller than the terminal, Ink scrolled it, and the first visible line was the tail of a row: the notice — the sentence saying why the screen is open and what to do about the missing credential — the title and the count were above the top. The box is now sized from the lines it will actually draw, counted from the same wrapped strings it renders, and the list is windowed to what is left with the window following the cursor; at 50 columns the `N detected · …` summary is the one line that gives way, since it says nothing the rows below it do not. The `← current` mark is counted in the row's width, and moves below the source when sharing the line would leave the source nine columns. `(free)` printed twice on 22 of the 25 zero-priced rows of the catalogue this was measured against, because that catalogue names its free variants `… (free)`; the marker stays and the repetition goes, decided by the word rather than by one vendor's punctuation, so `[FREE]` and `free tier` count and `Freehand` does not. Two smaller things: the notice and every other sentence the box is sized against are wrapped by the screen rather than by Ink, whose wrapping keeps the space it broke at, and the footer now says what `esc` does — `esc cancel` returns to the session behind the picker, `esc exit namzu` closes the program, which is what `handlePickerCancel` does when there is nothing behind it. The page records the windowing, the wrapping source column and the two esc meanings.
## 2026-09-18

- **Update** [Provider credentials and private state](cli/credentials.md), whose picker section now describes rows grouped by vendor rather than one row per provider id. The defect: one vendor could be two rows, because the registry models a subscription sign-in and an API key as two ids — a machine with the subscription signed in drew `OpenAI (Codex subscription)` on one line and, below it under `Not detected — enter a credential to use these:`, the same vendor asking for `OPENAI_API_KEY`, so the operator holding the key had to work out which of the two rows was theirs. A row is now a vendor, and the ways in are held inside it: the detected session first, then the API key, then — where nothing works yet — a sign-in the registry declares. `ProviderRegistryEntry` gained a required `vendor` field, because which ids are one vendor is a fact about the providers and the picker is only the first surface to need it; `VENDOR_NAMES` writes each vendor's name once and `VendorId` is derived from it, so a `vendor` naming a vendor that is not named there does not compile. Zen and Zen Go deliberately stay two rows — one company's two services, with separate catalogues, separate billing routes and keys that do not open each other — because merging those would put a product decision inside a screen about credentials; Ollama and LM Studio are two local servers for the same reason. Enter keeps the meaning it has always had wherever a row's ways in name ONE provider, which is every other row on the screen: an unconfigured row still opens its paste field and a detected one still opens its models, so the daily surface is unchanged keystroke for keystroke. It asks only where the row's paths name more than one provider, and in this registry that is the one vendor whose two ids are a subscription and a key. A sign-in inside a vendor row is not a second mechanism: it is the operation `l` starts, written once and reached from both places, and the API key still opens the paste field whose value travels `setKeyEntry` → `acceptKey` → the session credential. Nothing about credential resolution at run time changed and no config key was added. One structural consequence is recorded on the page because it is visible: nine vendors is the most this list can draw, so the digit shortcut's nine-row limit is now a bound the screen cannot pass rather than one it can overshoot, and the sentence about rows past nine stays for the day a tenth vendor exists. Four test fixtures that build a registry entry by hand needed the new field — the same lesson their own comments already record about `constructible`, one field earlier. The grouping and the path order are decided in `provider-list.test.ts`, where they are pure; `__tests__/a-vendor-row-offers-every-way-in.test.tsx` drives the screen, and five of its eight cases fail against the previous sources.
- **Update** [Provider credentials and private state](cli/credentials.md), which now describes the provider picker's list and the credential entry on it — the screen this change alters. The picker listed what discovery found, so a provider the operator had saved and held a key for but had not exported as an environment variable appeared nowhere on the screen that exists to choose one: no row to select, therefore no way to type the key. Every provider this build can construct that takes a typed credential is now on it, appended below the detected rows under `Not detected — enter a credential to use these:` and marked with the variable it needs (`needs OPENROUTER_API_KEY`). `k` follows the highlighted row rather than a fixed choice in the registry, the digits still reach the first nine rows with the arrows covering the rest, and the page records the four omissions with their reasons — Bedrock's credential chain, `http`'s missing base URL, LM Studio's absent driver, and Codex, which is a device-code sign-in rather than a key. The page states one thing a reader would otherwise assume the other way: a credential typed here is held for the session, and the variable named on the row is what makes it durable.
- **Update** [Request-only step context](sdk/step-context.md) and [Answer verification](sdk/verification.md), for a deadline that is no longer a deadline. Both pages said the bounded auxiliary inference's `timeoutMs` defaults to and cannot exceed 10,000 ms; neither said what enforcing it costs. It costs the run: an aborted request has no final usage receipt, so the shared ledger retained it unresolved, and an account with unresolved spend admits nothing further — `beforeIteration` reads `remaining` as 0 and stops the run with `token_budget`. Measured against the real free OpenRouter model in the CLI, the auxiliary call took 17.3 s, the deadline fired at 10.0 s, and the run stopped 10.0 s after the operator's message with "usage for a model request could not be confirmed" — in every turn after the first, before the model was asked, so no tool call in the run could execute. Both pages now state that `timeoutMs` is accepted, validated, deprecated and no longer bounds the request, that the call is bounded by the provider's own request timeout and the run's cancellation instead, and that a run-level `runConfig.timeoutMs` is what bounds the turn. The two bounds that remain are the ones every other model request in the run already has; the diagnostic and fail-open behaviour of an optional stage is unchanged.
