A new exported `MCPProtocolError` (`packages/sdk/src/connector/mcp/errors.ts`) carries `code: number` and `data?: unknown`; `error.message` still reads exactly `MCP error {code}: {message}` as before, so no existing log line or string-matching test is disturbed, and it is a subclass of `Error` so an existing catch site keeps working unchanged. Three new predicates — `isUnsupportedProtocolVersionError` (`-32022`), `isMissingRequiredClientCapabilityError` (`-32021`), `isHeaderMismatchError` (`-32020`) — let a caller react to a specific MCP protocol error without comparing magic numbers itself; a malformed error reply (a missing or non-integer `code`) rejects instead with a distinctly-named local error that none of the three match. A new `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND_CODES` constant (`packages/sdk/src/constants/mcp/index.ts`) lists the JSON-RPC codes a server may use for "that resource doesn't exist" (`-32602`, the legacy generic code, and `-32002`, the current spec's application-defined one). This is groundwork for the upcoming multi-era MCP client (issue #471): era negotiation, header recovery and the capability path all need to read a real error code and its `data`, which was previously unreachable. `docs/sdk/mcp-protocol-eras.md` does not exist yet — a later workstream in the same effort creates it. `.changeset/mcp-typed-protocol-errors.md`, **minor** for `@namzu/sdk` — the new error class and predicates are additive public surface.

- **Creation** Add [MCP content blocks](sdk/mcp-content-blocks.md) and widen the exported `MCPContentBlock` union (`packages/sdk/src/types/connector/mcp.ts`) with `audio` (`{ type: 'audio', data, mimeType }`, since 2025-03-26) and `resource_link` (`{ type: 'resource_link', uri, name, description?, mimeType? }`, since 2025-06-18), plus optional `blob?` and `annotations?` (new `MCPContentAnnotations`) on the existing `resource` variant — these block types were previously silently dropped by `mcpToolResultToToolResult` (`packages/sdk/src/connector/mcp/adapter.ts`), even for a server already negotiated to a later era. `audio` is admitted through a new `audio-admission.ts` (mirroring `image-admission.ts`'s atomic-batch, structural-container-check pattern for `audio/wav`, `audio/ogg` and `audio/mpeg`); an admitted clip is named by its media type in `output` rather than passed through as bytes, since `ToolResultBlock` has no audio carrier yet (documented as a known gap on the new page). A malformed audio batch is withheld with a notice in the same shape as the existing image-withheld notice. `resource_link` is rendered as a single named-pointer text block (`[MCP resource link: <name> (<uri>)]`), consistent with how a URI-only embedded `resource` has always been handled — named or omitted, never fabricated. A `resource` carrying `blob` and no `text` no longer risks being mishandled; it is left out of model-visible content without throwing, same as a URI-only resource, while the raw block (blob included) still reaches `ToolResult.data`. `annotations` on a `resource` are carried through untouched into `data`; namzu does not act on them. The working-state pin path (`WORKING_STATE_MIME`) is unchanged. `.changeset/mcp-content-blocks.md`, **minor** for `@namzu/sdk` — flags explicitly that widening this exported OUTPUT union can break a consumer's exhaustive `switch` at compile time even though nothing is removed at the value level.

- **Update** Fix the [Sandbox contract conformance suite](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md#running-the-conformance-suite)'s `openTcpConnection` positive case (`packages/sandbox/src/testing/sandbox-conformance.ts`): it started its echo listener on the orchestrator/test process's own loopback, which only ever proved anything for a backend whose "guest" happened to share that loopback with the process running the suite — never for a real remote sandbox, which cannot dial it at all. The case now starts its listener INSIDE the sandbox through `openTerminal` (`node -e`, reporting the port it bound on its own stdout) and dials that back through `openTcpConnection`, working identically for the two colocated fixtures and a real remote guest. New optional fields on `SandboxConformanceOptions`, `guestCanRunNode` and `guestListenerCommand`, let a backend whose guest cannot run a listener this way skip the case with a stated reason instead of failing it. Confirmed in-cluster: `research/k8s-sandbox/kind-e2e-results.md`'s 2026-09-16 addendum re-runs the suite against a live kubernetes acquisition, where the case previously failed with `connect ECONNREFUSED` and now passes. Also corrects a self-contradictory `@namzu/sdk` doc comment on `Sandbox.openTerminal` that told an implementer to both "throw" and "omit" for a guest that cannot provide one; it now says only "omit", matching `Sandbox.openTcpConnection`'s own wording (comment-only, no behavior or type changed). `.changeset/conformance-tcp-remote.md`, **patch** for `@namzu/sandbox` — this suite is not published from the package's own entry point (no `testing` subpath in `@namzu/sandbox`'s `exports` map), and the added options are additive and optional.

- **Creation** Add [portable tool schemas](sdk/tool-schema-portability.md): the kernel now renders every tool schema in the INTERSECTION of JSON Schema draft-07 and 2020-12, so no driver has to convert a dialect for it. `read`'s `readRange` was the only `z.tuple` in the first-party tool surface and rendered as the draft-07 tuple `items: [a, b]`, which a wire validating `parameters` against the 2020-12 metaschema refuses — rejecting the whole request, every other tool in it included. It is now a length-2 array of positive integers; `[10, 40]` is still exactly what a model writes and what the parser accepts. New on the SDK's public surface: `findPortableSchemaViolations`, `toPortableToolSchema`, `toolWireSchema` and `PortableSchemaViolation`. A bridged MCP tool's positional array now reaches the model as a uniform array plus a description naming each position, rather than as `prefixItems`.

- **Fix** Admit the ACI standby-pool backend through `@namzu/sandbox`'s exported `SandboxProviderConfig` union (`packages/sandbox/src/index.ts`): `ACIStandbyPoolBackendConfig` was already a member of `SandboxBackendConfig` and fully implemented, but missing from `SandboxProviderConfig`, so `createSandboxProvider` accepted a `container` / `aci-standby-pool` config only by lying to the type checker, and `pickBackend` read it back out through two `as unknown as` casts. `SandboxProviderConfig` now carries a real arm for it (paired with the `ContainerSandboxLayout` it requires, same as the plain container arm), and `pickBackend` narrows to it by the `runtime: 'aci-standby-pool'` discriminant with no cast, checked ahead of the `docker` default so that branch's own narrowing is undisturbed. No runtime behavior changed — only the public type now admits a shape the runtime already accepted.

- **Creation** Add [Kubernetes sandboxes](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md): `@namzu/sandbox` gains a `microvm` / `kubernetes` backend that claims VM-isolated sandboxes out of an [agent-sandbox](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/agent-sandbox) `SandboxWarmPool`, with a pool-less direct `Sandbox` fallback. The page names the new exported `KubernetesBackendConfig` and `KubernetesClusterAccess`, the pristine-claim rule the sub-second acquire depends on (a claim carrying `spec.env` or `spec.volumeClaimTemplates` is forced to cold-start upstream, so neither is ever set), why the bound sandbox's name is read back out of `status.sandbox` instead of assumed, the wall-clock `shutdownTime` that bounds every created object against a host crash, and the per-instance agent credential (the pod's own `metadata.uid`). It also states plainly what this first batch does NOT carry: the execution surface (`exec`, file IO and terminals throw a named error today), workspace suspend/resume, egress mapping and the cluster manifests.

- **Update** Extend [Kubernetes sandboxes](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md#egress) with an egress section: the new config-level `egress` block (`policy`, `networkPolicyName`, `engine`) translates `deny-all`/`allow-all` into a `NetworkPolicy` that always leaves the cluster's own DNS reachable, and refuses `static`/`resolver` hostname allowlists at construction — core `NetworkPolicy` has no FQDN concept — unless `engine: 'cilium'` is declared, in which case it emits a `CiliumNetworkPolicy` with `toFQDNs`. Documents the new `sandbox.namzu.ai/template` pod label every Sandbox this backend creates carries (agent-sandbox's own controller-owned template label is written only on a Sandbox adopted out of a pool, never on one this backend POSTs directly) and the requirement that a `SandboxWarmPool`'s own `SandboxTemplate` carry the same label so pooled sandboxes match the translated policy too. States the verify-not-trust contract: the first `create()` after construction `GET`s the applied `NetworkPolicy`/`CiliumNetworkPolicy` and refuses to proceed on a 404 or a shape mismatch, naming the fix, rather than trusting an object exists because a name matches — this backend never creates the policy itself, the same operator-applies-the-boundary precedent as the docker backend's network. Adds `get` on `networkpolicies`/`ciliumnetworkpolicies` to the RBAC section, conditional on `config.egress`.

- **Update** Give [Kubernetes sandboxes](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md#the-sandbox-surface) its execution surface, an acquire-time privilege probe and a lease. `create()` now returns a real `Sandbox` over the guest agent instead of one whose `exec`, file IO and terminals threw a named not-wired-yet error: `exec` (cancellation terminates the guest process and the peer confirms it), `writeFile`/`readFile`, `listFiles`, `openTerminal` (killed and awaited by `destroy()`) and `openTcpConnection`. `setNetworkPolicy`, `spawnDetached` and `walkFiles` are absent on purpose and the page's new capability table says why for each — the SDK's contract makes omitting a capability the honest answer and accepting one this backend cannot enforce the dishonest one. The page also documents the per-call write-file ceiling (every request dials fresh, so every request is an unauthenticated first frame and is bounded by the guest's 8 MiB pre-auth limit; an oversized body throws the now-exported `AgentPreauthFrameTooLargeError` before dialing), the probe (`cat /proc/self/status` through `execute`, admitting only all-four-masks-zero plus `NoNewPrivs: 1`, refusing a probe that could not run in words that distinguish it from a privileged guest, destroying the instance so no handle escapes, with no off switch and a clock of its own — `min(readyTimeoutMs, 15s)`, so a guest that accepts the connection and then goes quiet is refused instead of holding `create()` open for the execution controller's five-minute generic default), and the lease (the handle merge-PATCHes `shutdownTime` forward every half TTL, jittered ±10%; `destroy()` stops it, a failed renewal — an individual PATCH that hangs included, since every tick carries its own deadline — is reported to the new `onLeaseRenewalError` config callback and retried, and an object found already deleted marks the handle gone). New exported errors: `KubernetesPrivilegeProbeError`, `KubernetesSandboxDestroyedError`, `KubernetesSandboxGoneError`, `KubernetesAgentUnauthorizedError`. RBAC now needs `patch` on `sandboxclaims`/`sandboxes` for the renewal.

- **Creation** Add a [Sandbox contract conformance suite](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md#running-the-conformance-suite) to `@namzu/sandbox`: `defineSandboxConformance` (`packages/sandbox/src/testing/sandbox-conformance.ts`) asserts the SDK's `Sandbox` contract — `exec` exit codes and streamed output, the `AbortSignal` contract (the process is genuinely terminated, never a resolved result that reads as an unaborted success), a `writeFile`/`readFile` round trip including binary content, `listFiles`, `openTerminal` ownership on `destroy()`, `openTcpConnection` to guest loopback and its refusal of a non-loopback host, destroy idempotence, and every call failing once destroyed — against a caller-supplied `describe`/`it`/`expect` and a factory producing a fresh `Sandbox`, the same shape `@namzu/sdk/testing`'s checkpoint-store and provider-driver suites already use. `openTerminal` and `openTcpConnection` are optional on the SDK's contract, so a factory whose sandbox omits either capability skips that section rather than failing it. It runs green against both the kubernetes backend (a real `agent/agent.cjs` on a loopback TCP socket) and the Firecracker backend (the same agent over its existing unix-domain-socket fixture) in this same change — passing against two independently-built backends is what makes it a contract suite rather than one backend's tests wearing a new name. Its own negative test (`packages/sandbox/src/testing/__tests__/conformance-fails-a-broken-sandbox.test.ts`) proves three deliberately broken `Sandbox`s each fail the suite by name: one that resolves `exec` after abort instead of terminating the process, one whose `destroy()` leaves a terminal running, and one whose `readFile` hands back corrupted bytes. Not published from `@namzu/sandbox`'s own entry point or from `@namzu/sdk/testing` — the package has no `testing` subpath yet, and a caller inside the monorepo imports the suite by relative path, as both new test files do.
- **Update** Give [Kubernetes sandboxes](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md#persistent-workspaces) a persistent workspace: a `Sandbox` that keeps a block-mode disk across a suspend. New on the public surface: `createKubernetesWorkspace(config, options)`, the `KubernetesWorkspace` type (the SDK's `Sandbox` plus `suspend()`, `resume()`, a `suspended` flag and a `destroy()` widened with `deleteDisk`), `KubernetesWorkspaceOptions`, `KubernetesWorkspaceDestroyOptions`, `KubernetesWorkspaceTransitionOptions`, `KubernetesWorkspaceDiskError`, `KubernetesWorkspaceMismatchError`, `KubernetesWorkspaceSuspendTimeoutError` and `KubernetesWorkspaceSuspendedError` — all in `@namzu/sandbox`, with `@namzu/sdk`'s `Sandbox` untouched. The page states the three things a caller has to know before relying on one: the disk is fixed at creation (`volumeClaimTemplates` is CEL-immutable and a claim carrying one cold-starts, so "attach a disk later" is not expressible in the API), there is no delete-compute-keep-disk verb so `destroy()` DEFAULTS to suspending and only `destroy({ deleteDisk: true })` removes the files, and a resume changes both the address and the agent token so nothing from before a suspend is reused. It also documents the refusal of any workspace template whose disk is not `volumeMode: Block` consumed through `volumeDevices` (a `Filesystem` PVC under a VM-isolating RuntimeClass reaches the guest over virtio-fs and is quietly several times slower at the small-file IO a workspace is made of), that a workspace carries no `shutdownTime` and no renewal loop, and that calling the verb twice reattaches to the existing workspace under its deterministic `namzu-ws-<id>` name. `config.egress` applies to a workspace exactly as it does to a task sandbox: the same synchronous refusal of a hostname allowlist the declared engine cannot enforce, and the same verify-not-trust `GET` of the applied `NetworkPolicy` before anything is created — run against the template the workspace is actually built from, whose label is the one the policy's `podSelector` has to match. A suspend waits on the POD rather than on the Sandbox's `Suspended` condition (upstream leaves that condition True after a resume) or a `deletionTimestamp` (set while the guest is still running and still writing), and a resume polls the pod's uid under the same deadline until it differs from the one the last landed suspend patch retired, because `Ready` is left standing across the transition too and a token read off a status that has not caught up is the old pod's. Both verbs record a state only once the cluster has confirmed it — `suspended` after the patch lands AND the pod is observed stopped, `deleted` after the DELETE resolves or reports the object already gone — since each is idempotent by early-returning on that record, and writing it on the way out turns a failed request into a permanent silent success: a `destroy({ deleteDisk: true })` whose DELETE 500s would answer every retry "already deleted" while the Sandbox, its pod and its PVC stood on the cluster, and a `suspend()` whose patch 500s would leave a pod running behind a handle that said it was asleep. A failed request leaves the state it found and rethrows; a second `suspend()` or `destroy()` arriving mid-transition awaits the one in flight, under the first caller's `signal`, rather than sending a second request into the window that deferral opens. `destroy()` stays idempotent across its two shapes as well: a plain `destroy()` on a workspace `destroy({ deleteDisk: true })` already removed is a no-op in either admission order, since `destroy()` is the verb a `finally` block calls, while an explicit `suspend()` on a deleted workspace still throws. Reattaching to an existing object checks that object rather than the caller's intention: the block disk, the `sandbox.namzu.ai/template` pod label (what an egress policy's `podSelector` matches, so an object built from another template is refused even though the policy verified) and `runtimeClassName` when one is configured (the VM boundary, which the privilege probe cannot see go missing) — a disagreement throws `KubernetesWorkspaceMismatchError` before the object is woken or dialed, and nothing is patched to make it match. No failure path deletes, and that includes the one nobody calls: when an execution's cancellation cannot be confirmed — a wedged agent, a partitioned pod — the shared execution controller retires the pod the command was left in, and where a task sandbox is retired by being DELETEd (its object is disposable), a workspace is retired by the same `operatingMode: Suspended` patch, so the `exec()` rejects carrying its `retirement` observation, the workspace reads `suspended: true` and `resume()` brings up a fresh pod on the same disk. Fixes a defect on the task path in the same change: a pool-less `create()` copied the `SandboxTemplate`'s `podTemplate` and dropped its `volumeClaimTemplates`, producing a healthy Sandbox with no disk.

- **Creation** Add the [deployment](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md#deployment) artifacts everything above assumed but the repo did not yet ship: `packages/sandbox/k8s/` (never published — the package's `files` array packs only `dist` and `src`; `npm pack --dry-run` confirms it). `k8s/Dockerfile` builds a guest image on the same Debian-bookworm-slim lineage as the existing container-tier reference image, adding only `util-linux` (`setpriv`, `blkid`) and `e2fsprogs` (`mkfs.ext4`) to a lean base — deliberately NOT that fat reference image, since the guest agent's `exec` already carries whatever a task pulls in, and baking a doc-gen toolchain into every warm-pool replica this tier churns through would be pure pull/refill cost paid by every deployment that never uses it. `k8s/entrypoint.sh` (POSIX sh, root): for a workspace's raw block device, `blkid`s it and runs `mkfs.ext4 -F` ONLY when it reports no filesystem — an unconditional `mkfs` would erase a resumed workspace's disk on every boot, the single most destructive possible bug this batch could ship — then mounts `noatime,nodev,nosuid` and `chown`s it; a task pod names no device and skips the whole step. Either way it finishes by `export`ing `NAMZU_SANDBOX_WORKSPACE` to match wherever it just decided the workspace root is, then `exec`s straight into `setpriv --reuid --regid --clear-groups --inh-caps=-all --bounding-set=-all --no-new-privs -- node agent.cjs` — the exact sequence `privilege-probe.ts` verifies on every acquire rather than trusts from this file. One container, no init container, no sidecar, no `mountPropagation`. Manifests under `k8s/manifests/`: `runtimeclass.yaml` (with a loud comment that the registered Kata name has drifted between published sources and must be read off `kubectl get runtimeclass`, never trusted from this repo), `sandboxtemplate-task.yaml` and `-workspace.yaml` (the backend-owned `sandbox.namzu.ai/template` pod label, `securityContext.privileged` for the entrypoint's mount step, `NAMZU_AGENT_BIND_TOKEN` from the downward API's `metadata.uid` — never a literal, never shared across pooled replicas — and an inline `networkPolicy` block agent-sandbox's own controller enforces as an EARLIER, independent layer on top of the standalone policy below), `sandboxwarmpool.yaml` (`updateStrategy: OnReplenish`, with a comment on `Recreate`'s PVC-cascade risk for a disk-bearing pool), `networkpolicy.yaml` (a standalone, operator-applied core `NetworkPolicy` — the PRIMARY boundary this page's agent-credential section already named: default-deny ingress except the host's own pods on port 1024, egress DNS plus a placeholder allowed-destinations list, selecting every sandbox this backend creates by the template label's mere EXISTENCE rather than one template's value, so one object covers both templates), and `rbac.yaml` (a namespaced `Role` — no wildcard verb, no `ClusterRole` — granting exactly the verbs `../kubernetes-sandbox.md#rbac`'s RBAC section already documented, plus `get` on `sandboxwarmpools` for one acceptance script's own readiness check, named as the exception it is). A `kind-overlay/` Kustomize overlay drops `runtimeClassName` and converts the workspace disk to `Filesystem` mode for local development against a stock kind cluster, which has neither Kata nor Block-capable dynamic provisioning — loudly documented as NOT a security boundary. Five scripts under `k8s/scripts/` (`contract-suite.mjs`, `acquire-p50.mjs`, `suspend-resume.mjs`, `io-compare.mjs`, `capability-check.mjs`) each measure one acceptance criterion against a live cluster and print a pass/fail line plus the measured number; `k8s/scripts/lib/cluster-access.mjs` is their shared, dependency-free `--in-cluster`/`{server,ca,getToken}` plumbing. `agent/agent.cjs` gains its ONE permitted change here: a `SIGTERM` handler. `entrypoint.sh` `exec`s straight into the agent, so in a real pod it is pid 1 of the container's own pid namespace, and Linux leaves a signal whose default action is "terminate" un-applied for pid 1 with no handler installed — a kind smoke run confirmed exactly that: an unhandled `SIGTERM` did nothing and the pod rode out its full `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` before `SIGKILL`. The handler closes the listener and calls `process.exit(0)` — no drain, the same "gone" a caller already handles from a pod the cluster removed out from under it. Also states the `exec` [cancellation semantics](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md#the-sandbox-surface) plainly for the first time: a confirmed cancellation resolves with the terminal signal/exit code, exactly as the Firecracker tier's `exec` does. `k8s/__tests__/entrypoint.test.ts` and `manifests.test.ts` run in the package's ordinary `pnpm test` — no cluster, no root — by PATH-shimming every tool `entrypoint.sh` calls and, for the one check that needs a real block-special file (`-b` is a shell built-in no `PATH` shim can fake), `stat()`ing an already-existing `/dev/loopN` node rather than creating one. `.changeset/k8s-cluster-artifacts.md`, **minor** bump for `@namzu/sandbox` (the guest agent gained an additive capability, not merely a doc/tooling change).

- **Fix** Lower the [Kubernetes/Firecracker guest agent](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md#the-sandbox-surface)'s cancel-confirm grace default (`NAMZU_AGENT_CANCEL_GRACE_MS`, `packages/sandbox/agent/agent.cjs`) from `2000`ms to `250`ms. `terminateAndConfirm` escalated a cancelled `exec`'s `SIGTERM` to `SIGKILL` only if the owned process group was still alive at the end of this window, with nothing checking that a since-quiet group went quiet BECAUSE of the signal rather than by finishing on its own — so a process that traps and ignores `SIGTERM` but happens to complete before the window elapsed ran to completion untouched and was reported back as a clean, unaborted-looking success, in violation of `SandboxExecOptions.signal`'s contract ("must terminate the owned process ... never silently ignore the signal and let the command run to completion"). Every existing suite that drives this path overrode the grace to 50ms "to prove the kill in milliseconds, not the production window," which happened to flip which side of that race won against the shared conformance fixture's ~400ms-to-finish ignoring command and hid the shipped default's own behavior from every test. The new default leaves that fixture a wide margin while still giving a fast, well-behaved `SIGTERM` handler room to clean up; a deployment that genuinely needs a longer cooperative-shutdown window sets the variable explicitly, as before — this narrows the race rather than closing the underlying gap, which a future change may address by checking the child's actual exit reason instead of a timing window. `.changeset/kubernetes-agent-cancel-grace-default.md`, **patch** bump for `@namzu/sandbox`. Also disclose, in the same changeset and in [Kubernetes sandboxes](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md#the-sandbox-surface), a pre-existing, unfixed defect this default change makes somewhat easier to hit: the guest runs as the container's PID 1 with no subreaper, so a background job (`... &`) orphaned by a cancel-triggered `SIGKILL` reparents to the agent and is never reaped, and that execution's cancellation runs out the full cancel-confirm window and retires the sandbox instead of confirming — root-caused with in-cluster `/proc` evidence in `research/k8s-sandbox/kind-e2e-results.md` ("Defect 2", this date). Not yet fixed, and not yet filed as a repository issue.

- **Fix** Final round of the cancel-grace investigation above: mirror the SAME fix onto the container-tier worker, and mitigate Defect 2 instead of only documenting it. `packages/sandbox/worker/server.js`'s `terminateAndConfirm` had the identical unchecked-quiet-group race as `agent.cjs` (a review of the agent fix caught it existing verbatim here); `NAMZU_SANDBOX_CANCEL_GRACE_MS`'s default is now `250`ms too (was `2000`ms), with a regression test (`worker/__tests__/server.test.js`) that leaves it unset and fails against the old default exactly as the agent's own test does. **Defect 2 (the unreaped orphan) is now mitigated in the shipped Kubernetes image**, not merely a documented limitation: `packages/sandbox/k8s/Dockerfile` installs `tini`, and `k8s/entrypoint.sh`'s final `exec` is now `setpriv ... -- /usr/bin/tini -- node agent.cjs` — `tini` becomes the container's real PID 1 (a subreaper: it reaps a background job's grandchild orphaned by a cancel-triggered `SIGKILL`, which `agent.cjs`'s own `child_process` never would) with the agent as its child, forwarding `SIGTERM` to it exactly as before. `k8s/__tests__/entrypoint.test.ts` now asserts the exec chain names `tini` as the target with the agent as its own argv. [Kubernetes sandboxes'](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md#the-sandbox-surface) "Known limitation" paragraph is now "mitigated by `tini`," naming what a host building its own image (rather than `k8s/Dockerfile`) still has to provide itself; the privilege probe's documented expected entrypoint tail and its refusal error text both now name `tini` too (comment/error-text only, the probe's own checks are unchanged). Re-verified in-cluster against a freshly built `tini`-bearing agent image: the full conformance suite now reports **12/12** (`research/k8s-sandbox/abort-case-recheck.mjs` + `abort-case-runner.mjs`, checked in as a reproducible script this time, unlike the earlier 11/12 round which was recorded as prose only) — the `AbortSignal` case's own cancel-confirm time dropped from the previous round's 9637ms (the full 8s `RemoteExecutionController` window, because the orphaned background job could never be reaped) to 1502ms, in line with every other case in the run. `.changeset/kubernetes-agent-cancel-grace-default.md` (same changeset, extended rather than superseded) stays a **patch** for `@namzu/sandbox`: `agent.cjs`, `worker/server.js`, the k8s `Dockerfile` and `entrypoint.sh` are all guest/worker/deployment files outside the published tarball, and both changes only tighten when a cancellation escalates or gets reaped — no public type, export, or documented default for a consumer changed.

- **Update** Give [delegated-work](cli/delegated-work.md) agent rows a resolved model and live token/tool-call counters. `SubagentActivity` gains optional `model`, `tokens` and `toolCalls`; `SubagentActivityMonitor.begin()` takes an optional `model` and the `Agent` tool's execute (`packages/cli/src/integrations/subagents/runtime.ts`) passes the same `selection?.model ?? fileAgent?.model ?? opts.model` it already resolves for the child definition. A new `token_usage_updated` projection sets `tokens` from the event's cumulative `usage.totalTokens`, never `contextTokens` — a different, shrinking quantity the event's own doc comment distinguishes for exactly this reason. `toolCalls` increments once per `tool_executing`, not again on `tool_completed`. `AgentExplorer.tsx` renders both in the automatic rail, the agent cockpit and the child transcript header, compacted to `42.1k`/`1.38M` plus a `· N tools` suffix; an agent that has not yet reported usage shows an em dash rather than `0`. Existing width breakpoints drop the counters first, then the model name, and never truncate the description to make room; a resolved model id is host-reported, unbounded text (a self-hosted or gateway-style id can run 60-90+ cells), so the label itself is capped to a short budget with an ellipsis before it is placed next to the description, instead of an unbounded label forcing the description down to zero width in the automatic rail and the transcript header. `.changeset/agent-rows-counters.md`, **minor** bump for `@namzu/cli` — `SubagentActivity` is internal to the CLI, not exported from its public entry, so this adds operator-visible capability without changing any published type.

- **Update** Give [delegated-work](cli/delegated-work.md) phases an optional detail text, revealed for the focused phase. The `Agent` tool's input schema (`packages/cli/src/integrations/subagents/runtime.ts`) gains `phase_detail`, described in the same display-only words as `workflow`/`phase`/`phase_order` — it creates no dependencies, barriers or serial execution. `SubagentActivityMonitor.begin()` (`packages/cli/src/integrations/subagents/activity.ts`) threads it into the monitor's per-phaseId record first-writer-wins, the same rule `phaseOrder` already follows, so a later sibling declaring a different detail cannot make the pane flicker; it is bounded with the existing `normalizedLabel`/`bounded` helpers at `MAX_AGENT_ACTIVITY_LABEL_CODE_UNITS`. New optional `phaseDetail` field on `SubagentActivity` and `detail` on `AgentExplorer.tsx`'s `AgentPhase`, populated in `agentPhases()`. `PhasePane` renders it beneath the phase list for the phase carrying the `›` cursor only — the others show none — wrapped to the pane width and clipped to a fixed 3-line budget in a box whose height never changes with the text's length; a phase with no detail renders exactly as before, with no reserved blank space. In the cockpit's compact layout (a terminal under 20 rows) the detail band stays hidden entirely — the same layout already drops the pane's other secondary text, and there is no spare row to give it without squeezing the agent pane out of the frame. Phase identity stays the monitor-owned `phaseId`; the detail never affects it. `permission-review.ts`'s compact approval plan and detailed pager both surface `phase_detail` now, so the plan an operator approves and the cockpit they later inspect agree. `.changeset/phase-detail-label.md`, **minor** bump for `@namzu/cli`.

- **Update** Make a delivered [`send_message`](cli/delegated-work.md) correction visible on both sides of the exchange, not only as a one-line "queued" acknowledgement that scrolled away while the child's transcript silently jumped to a redirected next turn. `SubagentActivityMonitor.recordMessage(taskId, text, direction)` (`packages/cli/src/integrations/subagents/activity.ts`) pushes a bounded row through the existing `pushRow` path used by every other transcript row, tagged with a new optional `direction` field (`SubagentMessageDirection`, one value today: `'to-child'`) on the existing `system` row kind — no new row kind, no new `RunEvent`, no `TaskScheduler` change. The `Agent` tool's `send_message` execute (`packages/cli/src/integrations/subagents/runtime.ts`) calls it only after `gateway.continueTask` resolves, so a refused or unowned send — another parent's task, or one already finished — renders nothing on either side. `AgentExplorer.tsx` renders the row as `← from parent: …`, the arrow read from the receiving surface's point of view and drawn as the row's own glyph, not embedded in its text. The main conversation (`App.tsx`) gets a matching `<description> · correction sent` row with the message text beneath it, from a new ref-gated effect kept deliberately separate from the exactly-once terminal-agent announcement gate, so a later re-render or replay of the same snapshot cannot duplicate either side's row. `.changeset/visible-corrections.md`, **minor** bump for `@namzu/cli` — `recordMessage` and the row's `direction` field are internal to the CLI, not exported from its public entry.

- **Fix** Grant the shipped [Kubernetes sandbox host Role](sdk/kubernetes-sandbox.md#rbac) `get` on `ciliumnetworkpolicies` (`cilium.io`), matching the RBAC section's existing text. `packages/sandbox/k8s/manifests/rbac.yaml` granted `get` on `networkpolicies` (`networking.k8s.io`) only; a deployment with `config.egress.engine: 'cilium'` has `verifyEgressPolicyConfigured` read a `CiliumNetworkPolicy` instead, before every `createKubernetesWorkspace` and a provider's first `create()`, so the shipped Role 403'd on exactly the path the docs said it covered. A cluster with no Cilium CRDs installed never matches the added rule, so nothing changes for the default `'core'` engine. `k8s/README.md` now says so beside its egress/`networkpolicy.yaml` guidance, and `k8s/__tests__/manifests.test.ts` asserts the rule by name (still no wildcard verb, still a namespaced `Role`). `.changeset/sandbox-cilium-rbac.md`, **patch** for `@namzu/sandbox` — this manifest is not published in the npm tarball (`files` packs only `dist`/`src`), but it is what every documented deployment applies.

- **Fix** Protect [MCP's three canonical request headers](sdk/mcp-protocol-eras.md#typed-protocol-errors) in every era, not only the ones that emit headers of their own (`packages/sdk/src/connector/mcp/client.ts`). `requestAuthorityHeaders` derived its refused set from `buildEnvelope`'s own output for the request at hand, so a legacy connection older than `2025-06-18` — which sends no headers of its own — refused nothing: a caller-supplied `MCP-Protocol-Version`, `Mcp-Method` or `Mcp-Name` reached the wire unchanged on a 2024-11-05 or 2025-03-26 session, and `Mcp-Method`/`Mcp-Name` (headers this client never writes on any legacy connection) were unprotected on every legacy era, not only the two oldest — a gap the docs and the era-negotiation changeset already described as closed. The refused set (`CANONICAL_MCP_REQUEST_HEADERS`) is now fixed and matched case-insensitively regardless of what the era itself put on this request. New `per-request-authority.test.ts` cases connect a 2024-11-05 and a 2025-03-26 session and prove a forged header of each of the three names never reaches the wire, alongside the pre-existing modern-era and 2025-06-18+-legacy collision tests, which stay green. New `isResourceNotFoundError` predicate (`errors.ts`), exported through the usual `connector/mcp/index.ts` → `connector/index.ts` → `public-runtime.ts` chain, recognizing the two codes `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND_CODES` already listed (`-32002`, current spec; `-32602`, an older server's equivalent) but that nothing had consumed since the constant was added — one assertion per code plus the existing cross-predicate table's negative coverage, and a new "Typed protocol errors" note in `mcp-protocol-eras.md`. Also: `defaultMcpEraCache` is a module-level singleton several pre-existing MCP test files constructed `MCPClient` without overriding, so a resolved era from one `it()` could answer for a later one sharing the same transport identity — an order dependency `--sequence.shuffle` can expose. Every `MCPClient` construction across `packages/sdk/src/connector/mcp` now passes its own `eraCache: createMcpEraCache()`, and several in-memory harnesses gained an explicit `server/discover` reply so era resolution settles in a microtask rather than paying the 2s probe timeout once per test now that no test can free-ride on another's cached answer — the whole `connector/mcp` suite runs faster than before this change despite the added isolation. One pre-existing test had hardcoded the JSON-RPC id its `tools/list` request would get, true only while it shared a cache with an earlier test and never spent an id of its own on a probe; it now reads the id back from what was actually sent. New white-box `era-second-probe-retry.test.ts` exercises `resolveMcpEra`'s second-probe retry — a `-32022` naming a mutually-supported version other than the one first attempted — unreachable by any other test because `MCP_MODERN_VERSIONS` has exactly one entry in production; it mocks `constants/mcp/index.ts` to fabricate a second modern version for that one test file only (the real constant is untouched) and asserts exactly two probes, the second at the server-named version, with the cache holding that result afterward. `.changeset/mcp-header-authority-and-era-hardening.md`, **minor** for `@namzu/sdk` — `isResourceNotFoundError` is a new export; the header fix corrects behavior already documented as universal rather than changing what was promised.

- **Update** Document [the era probe's operator-visible cost](cli/mcp-servers.md#the-era-probe) and let a server override its own probe timeout (`packages/cli/src/integrations/mcp/servers.ts`). `mcp-modern-era-cli.md` already described the probe's cost as a CLI changeset, but the reference page never mentioned it: first contact with a given origin (HTTP) or resolved command (stdio) pays one extra `server/discover` round trip, cached afterward per server identity, and a legacy stdio server old enough to answer nothing on an unrecognized method pays the probe's own timeout once. New optional `eraProbeTimeoutMs` on `McpServerSpec`, validated exactly like `connectTimeoutMs` — refused rather than defaulted when given but not a positive number of milliseconds — and passed through to `new MCPClient(...)` only when the spec sets one, so an unconfigured server's behavior, and the SDK's own default and clamp, are unchanged. Lets an operator shorten the probe for a server known to be old and slow to connect, leaving more of `connectTimeoutMs` for the handshake that will actually answer. `.changeset/cli-era-probe-timeout.md`, **minor** for `@namzu/cli`.

## 2026-09-15

- **Update** Reorder the [owned delegated work projection](sdk/step-context.md#derived-work-context): `CompletionInbox.describeOwnedWork()` used to keep a single FIFO over every owned task, so a still-running task launched early fell out of the model's visibility once sixteen more tasks were merely LAUNCHED, whether or not any had finished. It now lists running tasks first (most recently launched first) and fills remaining slots with the most recently settled ones, still bounded to sixteen; a running task that does not fit is named honestly in the preamble ("and N more still running") instead of disappearing without a trace. Settled tasks bumped out are not individually counted — their result already reached the model once.

- **Update** Refresh two more citations in [cancellation and timeouts](sdk/cancellation-and-timeouts.md#todays-three-layers-briefly): the two `GuardCoordinator.beforeIteration` call sites enforcing `timeoutMs` are now at `packages/sdk/src/runtime/query/iteration/index.ts:474` and `:2786`, not `:473` and `:2733`.

- **Update** Correct the [whole-file read witness](sdk/step-context.md#derived-work-context) gate wording: a `read` witnesses itself when its window *covered the whole file* (`rendered.partial` false), not when `readRange`, `offset` and `limit` were all absent — a call that passes one of those and still reaches every line still witnesses. Aligns `docs/sdk/step-context.md`, `.changeset/full-read-witnesses.md` and `read-file.ts`'s own helper comment, which all said the stricter thing; no behavior changed.

- **Update** Tighten the [resume seed](sdk/tool-execution.md#file-changes-between-observation-and-edit)'s accounting and guards. A `write` a `pre_tool_use` hook SKIPPED is no longer replayed as a body that reached the disk — its receipt is a plain success, and both the replay and the projection now recognise the skip through the same function the executor writes it with. A read that withdraws a path stops counting toward the six-body eviction bound, so a later mutation cannot evict a path still holding a body. Attribution reads each call's `path` at most once per seeding and not at all past about a megabyte of arguments, which makes such a call a mutation that can be placed nowhere and seeds nothing. `query`'s checkpoint resume now seeds from the repaired history PLUS the part of an owned resume turn that a completed scan says already ran — a recovered write restores what it put there, an unknown outcome withdraws the path, and a call proved never started is left out because it is about to execute — and a seeding that throws is logged at debug and leaves the run resumable with the empty ledger it would have had.

- **Update** Correct two citations in [cancellation and timeouts](sdk/cancellation-and-timeouts.md#todays-three-layers-briefly): the `"is checked between agent iterations…"` quote is `streamIdleTimeoutMs`'s own doc comment contrasting itself against `timeoutMs`, not `timeoutMs`'s (`packages/sdk/src/types/run/config.ts:17-18`), and the `cancelled-provider-receipts.test.ts` describe/it pair is at 17-18, not 16-18.

- **Update** Recount [learning that a background job ended](cli/background-jobs.md#learning-that-it-ended) to four paths, not three: `deliverArrivedJobExits`, run as part of `settleOutstandingWork`, catches an awaited job's exit that lands after the suspend's grace already ran out but before the run finishes settling, delivering it instead of naming the job on `abandonedJobIds`. Names the window between that delivery and the CLI clearing `abortRef` (`App.tsx` ~4867) as a known, narrow case nothing announces.

- **Creation** Add a draft note on [cancellation and timeouts](sdk/cancellation-and-timeouts.md): the composition-order trap a naive whole-run deadline decorator would hit against retry and fallback, the CLI's own extra `AbortSignal` layer, the RAG embedding provider's separate timeout surface, the two independently-defaulted idle-timeout mechanisms, and the reader-cleanup inconsistency across fetch-based provider drivers. Findings and open options, not a committed design.

- **Update** Admit a whole-file `read` as [visible file evidence](sdk/step-context.md#derived-work-context), so a body the model already has in a receipt is referenced rather than read again. A `read` narrowed by nothing calls the new optional `FileReadTracker.recordFullRead`, which records the fingerprint of the rendering it returned alongside the ordinary whole-file observation, and `readWitness` reports it; the projection emits that call as a `kind: "read"` entry only while the receipt in the request still fingerprints to exactly what the tool emitted, under the same six-path cap and a 32,000-unit receipt bound. A windowed read witnesses nothing and still fingerprints the whole file, a read roots no edit chain, a write-rooted entry keeps a path both could claim, and a mutation refused for drift withdraws the entry as before. See [`recordFullRead`](sdk/tool-execution.md#file-changes-between-observation-and-edit).

- **Update** Rebuild the [file observation ledger](sdk/tool-execution.md#file-changes-between-observation-and-edit) at resume by replaying a conversation's own history, so a resumed run's [derived work context](sdk/step-context.md#derived-work-context) can reference files written before the session closed instead of re-reading them. New export `seedObservationLedger`; called by `resumeRun`, by `query`'s checkpoint resume from the repaired history, and by the CLI on the first turn of each conversation. The replay reads no file's content and restores a body only where history reconstructs it exactly, filing each entry under the canonical key the built-in tools key on so that a mutation-time drift refusal can still withdraw it; it never recovers a body from a `read` receipt — a read can confirm a reconstructed body by forward-rendering it through `read`'s own renderer, now shared from `tools/builtins/read-render.ts`, and otherwise withdraws it. A mutation the transcript left unanswered or refused withdraws the path it names rather than leaving a held body standing, so a drift refusal recorded in history is not undone by resuming. A path whose body it cannot reconstruct is left out of the ledger rather than entered without a fingerprint, so the read-before-overwrite refusal still stands over it and a seeded ledger is never weaker than the empty one a resume used to start from.

- **Update** Admit verified edit chains as [visible file evidence](sdk/step-context.md#derived-work-context): the observation ledger keeps a write's witness across the edits applied to it (`FileReadTracker.editChain`/`recordEdit` in [tool execution](sdk/tool-execution.md)), and the projection replays those calls through the tool's own apply core, admitting a path only when the replay matches the ledger's fingerprint, under an eight-call chain bound and a per-request ceiling of 262,144 UTF-16 code units, charged one operation at a time against a length worked out exactly from the body that operation is about to be applied to and checked before it is built. A mutation refused for drift now reports it through the optional `FileReadTracker.recordDriftObserved`, and the projection withdraws that path until the ledger's next content observation.

- **Update** Tidy the [awaited-job exit bookkeeping](cli/background-jobs.md#learning-that-it-ended): the record of an exit is dropped when the notice accounting for it is delivered, so a later job's notice cannot make a read exit hold a run open again; a hold takes the exits only together with the text that delivers them; and an awaited job that ends in the moment the run is settling is delivered on `Run.messages` rather than being lost between the kernel and the session.

- **Update** Suspend a run for [background jobs the model awaited](cli/background-jobs.md#waiting-at-the-end-of-a-turn): a turn that ends with no tool call now waits, for no tokens, on the exit of a job `wait_for_job` named, releasing on the exit, on operator input, or on the settle grace a delegated task gets under a two-minute ceiling of its own (`NAMZU_JOB_HOLD_MAX_MS`). Only an awaited job holds a run open, and one the grace ran out on is named on the run's `abandonedJobIds`.

- **Update** Add `wait_for_job` to [background jobs in the CLI](cli/background-jobs.md): blocks on a job's exit under a run/idle bound and returns its output in one call, instead of `job read` polled in a loop. `job`'s own description now points at it instead of instructing polling.

- **Update** Deliver the [repeat-call advisory](sdk/tool-execution.md#repeat-call-advisory) for structured tool results as its own `runtime-context` message after the batch, since `attachRepeatNotice`'s inline slot only ever existed for a plain-string result and silently dropped the notice otherwise.

## 2026-09-14

- **Update** Add bounded [derived work context](sdk/step-context.md#derived-work-context) for visible write evidence and owned worker outcomes, preserving mutation freshness checks and separating result delivery from user-facing completion.

- **Creation** Add [purpose-bound exploration policies](sdk/exploration-policies.md), preventing generated policies from changing their admitted purpose or entering ordinary task guidance, while retaining existing learning evaluation and rollback.

- **Creation** Add optional [environment exploration](sdk/resident-exploration.md) before resident skill generation, retaining observation evidence and usage through the existing SQLite journal and explicit CLI learning host.

- **Update** Classify [background job observation](cli/background-jobs.md) per action, support input-dependent read-only declarations in the [SDK tool builder](sdk/tool-execution.md), and preserve explicit [review rules](sdk/review-policy.md) through CLI compilation and durable review.

* **Update** Recorded a source-pinned [framework and computer-use gap audit](sdk/framework-gap-audit.md), desktop readiness probe, capability-advertising defect and AG-UI interoperability limits.

* **Update** Added [model-owned reasoning metadata](sdk/model-reasoning.md) and provider-neutral discovery for session effort controls.
* **Update** Interactive [model selection](cli/slash-commands.md) now continues to the selected model's effort menu; queued work waits for selection, and Escape keeps the selected model at its default effort.
* **Update**: [Tool execution barriers](/sdk/tool-execution.md), [Conversation evidence](/cli/conversation-evidence.md) and [Delegated work](/cli/delegated-work.md) — explicit SDK ordering, CLI write/verify boundaries, run-scoped retained evidence search, background delegation and queued child corrections.
* **Update**: [Slash commands](/cli/slash-commands.md) and [OpenAI reasoning menus](/sdk/openai-reasoning.md) — searchable models, direct host selection for standalone model requests, composer selection previews and exact Astra effort menus.
* **Update**: [Conversational model changes](/cli/slash-commands.md) and [Terminal design](/cli/terminal-design.md) — successful solitary switches settle without acknowledgement inference, repeated requests reuse acceptance, missing IDs receive bounded relevant choices, and empty deferred-tool search is withheld. [Harness efficiency review](/cli/harness-efficiency-review.md) records pinned upstream comparisons and measurable remaining gaps.
* **Update**: [Slash commands](/cli/slash-commands.md) and [Terminal design](/cli/terminal-design.md) — conversational model requests queue a session-only change after the active turn settles, resolve exact usable models, preserve history and identity, and confirm only successful application.
* **Update**: [Zen and Zen Go](/sdk/zen.md) — anonymous access defaults to public Muse Spark 1.3 Contributor Free, public discovery requires no installed client, and CLI credential lookup reuses separate OpenCode API-key entries without modifying their file; explicit public selection suppresses stored accounts, Responses tools preserve optional fields, and live public text inference is distinguished from catalogue and fixture coverage.

## 2026-09-07
* **Creation**: [Zen and Zen Go](/sdk/zen.md) — optional providers and CLI selection with exact native protocol routing, stable conversation attribution, validated reasoning replay, live catalogue admission and explicit transport and billing limits.
* **Creation**: [AG-UI clients](/sdk/ag-ui.md) — optional typed/SSE adapter with explicit host identity and history admission, backend tools, request-owned state, bounded events, final outcomes and documented CopilotKit compatibility limits.
* **Update**: [Harness invariants](/sdk/harness-invariants.md), [Token budgets](/sdk/token-budgets.md) and [Project state](/cli/project-state.md) — live delegation width releases completed history; bounded queued admissions preserve task ownership and reserve a parent coordination share.
* **Update**: [Terminal design](/cli/terminal-design.md) — independent workflows no longer become phases of earlier work; queued children, agent launch permissions and abnormal run stops have explicit displays.
* **Update**: [Terminal design](/cli/terminal-design.md) — the opening header keeps only brand/version; model and working directory appear in the footer, routine connection chatter is suppressed, and the composer owns the typing hint; contracted terminals rebuild the retained transcript, and subagent panes bound file-output wrapping and separate phase/task/status columns.
* **Update**: [Run exit codes](/cli/run-exit-codes.md) and [Run limits](/cli/run-limits.md) — buffered output uses the kernel's settled answer instead of concatenated narration and rejected candidates; streaming hosts receive that result as `done.text`.
* **Creation**: [Answer verification](/sdk/verification.md) — command/fingerprint errors and interrupted zero-exit checks cannot pass verification, cancellation reaches commands, and diagnostic clipping respects its character allowance; remaining completion-path limits are explicit.
* **Update**: [The salience-scored working set](/sdk/salience-working-set.md) — non-shrinking summary candidates are declined, useful staged clears survive, original clear/stub evidence is archived before replacement, and changed working-memory slots invalidate stale provider measurements.
* **Update**: [The salience-scored working set](/sdk/salience-working-set.md) — model changes resolve their own provider window, recheck compaction and use that denominator for telemetry and recovery; preparation order and remaining fit limits are explicit.
* **Update**: [Structured memory](/sdk/memory.md) — newly queued resume input takes precedence over old checkpoint intent, and operator steering updates the bounded state used by compaction.
* **Creation**: [Bounded code execution](/sdk/code-execution.md) — QuickJS interpreter isolation, explicit source/value/memory/call limits and optional structured nested-tool results with unchanged authorization.
* **Creation**: [Cognitive storage research](/sdk/cognitive-storage.md) — pinned Pydantic AI source comparison, proposed RAM/disk/index responsibilities, transactional update boundaries and a bounded local storage-cost experiment.
* **Creation**: [Cognitive architecture research](/sdk/cognitive-architecture.md) — primary-source motivation, functional definitions, proposed selective control cycle, scoped associative activation, outcome/evidence distinctions, local CPU measurements and executable SDK mechanism ablations; explicitly separate from shipped CLI behavior.
* **Update**: [Harness invariants](/sdk/harness-invariants.md) — advisory triggers use current context occupancy and the latest canonical tool failures rather than cumulative spend and stale error text.
* **Creation**: [Structured memory](/sdk/memory.md) — bounded per-step recall, current operator intent retained across compaction and checkpoints, ranked body search, explicit correction/archive/delete tools, and cooperating-process disk coordination with honest recovery limits.
* **Update**: [Memory](/cli/memory.md) and [Context and compaction](/cli/context-and-compaction.md) — default project recall with a file-config opt-out, one selected durable-memory writer, bounded scoped markdown reads and visible skipped-file or clipped-note diagnostics.
* **Update**: [Pinned facts](/sdk/pinned-facts.md) — removing the last tool pin clears its managed context slot; extracted negative requirements survive, assistant decisions retain source attribution, and bounded requirements disclose truncation.
* **Update**: [Ids](/sdk/ids.md) — `query` and `drainQuery` reject missing session, topic, project or tenant identity before provider calls, run events or persistence, including untyped JavaScript callers.
* **Update**: [The salience-scored working set](/sdk/salience-working-set.md) — verification retains rich-result text, tool-call chronology and attachment descriptors within one character budget, without copying encoded attachments or provider-private reasoning.
* **Update**: [Harness invariants](/sdk/harness-invariants.md) — full delegated results remain readable from their parent ledger after manager eviction, with foreign-parent and closed-runtime access refused.
* **Update**: [Terminal design](/cli/terminal-design.md) — long conversation names and goals use a cursor-following single-line editor, preserving the complete value through narrow terminals and resizing.
* **Creation**: [Run the kernel](/sdk/quick-start.md) — runnable offline SDK examples, explicit history and identity ownership, and agent-kernel positioning across the repository entry points.

## 2026-09-05
* **Update**: [Run limits](/cli/run-limits.md) — `--wait-for-provider <duration>` and `limits.waitForProviderMs`: a headless run waits out provider pauses and resumes from its checkpoint in the same process.
* **Creation**: [Tool servers](/cli/mcp-servers.md) — the `mcpServers` config key documented; `connectTimeoutMs` gives one server a longer connect deadline than the 10s default.
* **Creation**: [Exit codes of a headless run](/cli/run-exit-codes.md) — `namzu run` exits 75 (EX_TEMPFAIL) when the provider paused the run, so a wrapper can wait instead of retrying or giving up.
* **Creation**: [Pinned facts](/sdk/pinned-facts.md) — `ToolResult.workingState` and the MCP working-state resource; tools can now state facts into the working-memory slot.

## 2026-09-04
* **Creation**: [Memory](/cli/memory.md) — a project memory file beside the user one; `#note` and `/memory` write to the project by default; injected sections are capped.
* **Creation**: [Ids](/sdk/ids.md) — the `thd_` compatibility machinery is gone, retired prefixes are refused, a session can be created under a chosen id; the CLI mints its tenant and topic.
* **Creation**: [Run limits](/cli/run-limits.md) — `--max-iterations`, `--token-budget` and the `limits` config key; a headless run is no longer capped at a chat turn's 50 calls.

## 2026-09-02
* **Update**: [Background jobs in the CLI](/cli/background-jobs.md) — jobs run inside the sandbox now: the local provider starts them detached under the same confinement, the registry keeps them.
* **Creation**: [Adding a directory](/cli/add-dir.md) — `/add-dir`, `--add-dir`, `additionalDirectories`; the kernel's `query({ additionalDirectories })` reaches the tools and the sandbox.
* **Creation**: [File checkpoints](/cli/file-checkpoints.md) — every file recorded before a tool changes it, per turn; `/restore N` puts the tree back.
* **Creation**: [Slash commands](/cli/slash-commands.md) — every builtin and kernel command in one place; `/release-notes` is new.
* **Creation**: [Hook events](/sdk/hooks.md) — six more events (`user_prompt_submit`, `session_start`/`session_end`, `pre_compact`/`post_compact`, `subagent_stop`), the `annotate` result, and the shell hook contract for all ten; `/hooks` in the CLI.
* **Creation**: [The composer prefixes](/cli/composer-prefixes.md) — `!command` runs on the host without the model and the model reads the output next turn; `#note` remembers.
* **Update**: [Background jobs in the CLI](/cli/background-jobs.md) — a job is its process group: a command that backgrounds its work stays `running` until the survivor ends, and `kill` takes it.
* **Creation**: [Background jobs in the CLI](/cli/background-jobs.md) — session-owned jobs, exit notices for model and operator, `/jobs`.
* **Creation**: [Where the CLI stands against its peers](/cli/competitive-gaps.md) — the competitive survey and the backlog it orders.
* **Update**: consolidation landed — a run's learnings go to the memory store on request (`consolidateInto` in the kernel, `compaction.consolidate` in the CLI); the salience plan is complete.
* **Update**: compaction defaults to `salience` in the kernel and the CLI; `structured` stays selectable.
* **Update**: [The salience-scored working set](/sdk/salience-working-set.md) — the eval suite landed and two eviction rules with it; Phase 6's default flip and consolidation remain.
* **Creation**: [Context and compaction in the CLI](/cli/context-and-compaction.md) — the file-only `compaction` key and the `/context` command.
* **Update**: [The salience-scored working set](/sdk/salience-working-set.md) — phases 1–4 landed: scoring core, goal vector, working-set eviction, soft trigger; `strategy: 'salience'` is selectable.
* **Creation**: [The salience-scored working set](/sdk/salience-working-set.md) — the plan that makes per-message scoring and a dynamic context real; six phases, each with what it must prove.
* **Creation**: [The review policy](/sdk/review-policy.md) — the permission modes moved from the operator application into the kernel as `createReviewPolicy`.
* **Initialization**: `docs/` became an empty OKF v0.2 bundle. The pages written under the previous documentation standard were removed rather than migrated; a page returns when the code it describes is next touched, written to the bundle's rules.
